Moreover, there is a  ℤ \mathbb{Z} -subgroup of  Z ≤ π 1 ​ Σ 𝒜 Z\leq\pi_{1}\Sigma_{\mathcal{A}}  such that  π 1 ​ Σ 𝒜 = i ∗ ​ ( π 1 ​ U ^ H ) ⊕ Z \pi_{1}\Sigma_{\mathcal{A}}=i_{*}(\pi_{1}\widehat{U}_{H})\oplus Z . 
 
 
 
 
 2.7  Davis complexes 

 
 By a cell, we always mean a closed cell unless otherwise specified. 
 
 
 Definition 2.14   (Davis complex) . 
 
 Given a Coxeter group  W Γ W_{\Gamma} , let  𝒫 \mathcal{P}  be the poset of left cosets of spherical standard parabolic subgroups in  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  (with respect to inclusion) and let  b ​ Σ Γ b\Sigma_{\Gamma}  be the geometric realization of this poset (i.e.  b ​ Σ Γ b\Sigma_{\Gamma}  is a simplicial complex whose simplices correspond to chains in  𝒫 \mathcal{P} ). Now we modify the cell structure on  b ​ Σ Γ b\Sigma_{\Gamma}  to define a new complex  Σ Γ \Sigma_{\Gamma} , called the  Davis complex . The cells in  Σ Γ \Sigma_{\Gamma}  are induced subcomplexes of  b ​ Σ Γ b\Sigma_{\Gamma}  spanned by a given vertex  v v  and all other vertices which are  ≤ v \leq v  (note that vertices of  b ​ Σ Γ b\Sigma_{\Gamma}  correspond to elements in  𝒫 \mathcal{P} , hence inherit the partial order). 
 
 
 
 Suppose  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  is finite with  n n  generators. Then there is a canonical faithful orthogonal action of  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  on the Euclidean space  𝔼 n \mathbb{E}^{n} . Take a point in  𝔼 n \mathbb{E}^{n}  with trivial stabilizer, then the convex hull of the orbit of this point under the  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  action (with its natural cell structure) is isomorphic to  Σ 𝒜 \Sigma_{\mathcal{A}} , where  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  is the reflection arrangement associated with  W Γ W_{\Gamma} . In such case, we call  Σ Γ \Sigma_{\Gamma}  a  Coxeter cell . 
 
 
 The 1-skeleton of  Σ Γ \Sigma_{\Gamma}  is the unoriented Cayley graph of  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  (i.e. we start with the usual Cayley graph and identify the double edges arising from  s i 2 s^{2}_{i}  as single edges), and  Σ Γ \Sigma_{\Gamma}  can be constructed from the unoriented Cayley graph by filling Coxeter cells in a natural way. Each edge of  Σ Γ \Sigma_{\Gamma}  is labeled by a generator of  W Γ W_{\Gamma} . We endow  Σ Γ ( 1 ) \Sigma^{(1)}_{\Gamma}  with the path metric with edge length  1 1 . 
 
 
 
 2.8  Singular disk diagrams and combinatorial Gauss-Bonnet 

 
 We quickly review disk diagrams and the Gauss-Bonnet formula. We will only need the Gauss-Bonnet formula for piecewise flat simplicial complexes, though it is more natural to set it up in the more general context of combinatorial CW complexes. We refer to  [ MW02 , Definition 2.1]  for the definition of combinatorial CW complexes and combinatorial maps between them. We recall from  [ MW02 , Definition 2.6]  that a  (singular) disk diagram   D D  is a finite contractible 2-dimensional combinatorial CW complex with a fixed embedding in the plane  ℝ 2 \mathbb{R}^{2} . A  boundary cycle  of  D D  is a combinatorial map from a polygon  P P  to  D D  whose image is an edge-path in the graph  D ( 1 ) D^{(1)}  corresponding to going around  D D  once in the clockwise direction along the boundary of the unbounded complementary region  ℝ 2 ∖ D \mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus D  (see also  [ LS01 , p. 150] ). 
 
 
 Let  P → X P\to X  be a closed null-homotopic edge path in a 2-dimensional combinatorial CW complex  X X . A  singular disk diagram in  X X  for  P P 
is a singular disk diagram  D D  together with a map  D → X D\to X  such that the closed path
 P → X P\to X  factors as  P → D → X P\to D\to X  where  P → D P\to D  is a boundary cycle of  D D .
It is a theorem of Van Kampen that every null-homotopic closed edge path  P → X P\to X  is the boundary cycle of a singular disk diagram  D → X D\to X ; moreover, we can assume this singular disk diagram is  reduced , i.e.  D − D ( 0 ) → X D-D^{(0)}\to X  is an immersion, see  [ LS01 , V.2.1]  or  [ MW02 , Lemma 2.17] . We caution the reader that  D D  is usually not homeomorphic to a 2-dimensional disk (thus the name “singular” disk diagram), e.g. it is not if  P → X P\to X  is not an embedding.
Also even if  P → X P\to X  is an embedding, there might not exist a singular disk diagram for  P P  such that  D → X D\to X  is an embedding. If  X X  has a piecewise flat metric, then we equip the singular disk diagram  D D  with the natural piecewise flat metric induced by  D → X D\to X . 
 
 
 We will use the following version of the Gauss-Bonnet formula for a singular disk diagram  D D  which is a special case of the Gauss-Bonnet formula in  [ BB96 , Section 2] . We assume that  D D  has a piecewise flat structure. For a vertex  v ∈ D ( 0 ) v\in D^{(0)} , let  χ ⁡ ( v ) \chi(v)  be the Euler characteristic of  lk ⁡ ( v , D ) \operatorname{lk}(v,D) . Recall that the length of an edge of  lk ⁡ ( v , D ) \operatorname{lk}(v,D)  is the interior angle at  v v  of the 2-cell of  D D  corresponding to this edge. Let  α ⁡ ( v ) \alpha(v)  be the sum of the lengths of all edges in  lk ⁡ ( v , D ) \operatorname{lk}(v,D) .
Define  κ ⁡ ( v ) = ( 2 − χ ⁡ ( v ) ) ​ π − α ⁡ ( v ) \kappa(v)=(2-\chi(v))\pi-\alpha(v) . Then 
 

 
 
 ∑ v ∈ D ( 0 ) κ ⁡ ( v ) = 2 ​ π . \sum_{v\in D^{(0)}}\kappa(v)=2\pi. 
 
 (2.15) 
 
 
 
 Now suppose  X X  is a piecewise hyperbolic complex (e.g. each 2-cell of  X X  has the metric of a convex polygon in the hyperbolic plane).
Given a  2 2 -cell  C ∈ D ( 2 ) C\in D^{(2)} , we denote by  Area ⁡ ( C ) \mathrm{Area}(C)  the area of  C C . Then 
 

 
 
 ∑ v ∈ D ( 0 ) κ ⁡ ( v ) − ∑ C ∈ D ( 2 ) Area ⁡ ( C ) = 2 ​ π . \sum_{v\in D^{(0)}}\kappa(v)-\sum_{C\in D^{(2)}}\mathrm{Area}(C)=2\pi. 
 
 (2.16) 
 
 
 
 
 
 3  Preliminary II: Relative Artin complexes and their homotopy types 

 
 3.1  Artin complexes and relative Artin complexes 

 
 Recalled that the  Artin complex , introduced in  [ CD95a ]  and further studied in  [ GP12b ,  CMV20 ] , defined as follows. For each  s ∈ S s\in S , let  A s ^ A_{\hat{s}}  be the standard parabolic subgroup generated by  S ∖ { s } S\setminus\{s\} . Let  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma}  be the simplicial complex whose vertex set is corresponding to left cosets of  { A s ^ } s ∈ S \{A_{\hat{s}}\}_{s\in S} . Moreover, a collection of vertices span a simplex if the associated cosets have nonempty common intersection. It follows from  [ GP12b , Proposition 4.5]  that  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma}  is a flag complex. The Artin complex is an analogue of  Coxeter complex  in the setting of Artin group. The definition of a Coxeter complex  𝖢 Γ \mathsf{C}_{\Gamma}  of a Coxeter group  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  is almost identical to Artin complex, except one replaces  A s ^ A_{\hat{s}}  by  W s ^ W_{\hat{s}} , which is the standard parabolic subgroup of  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  generated by  S ∖ { s } S\setminus\{s\} . Each vertex of  𝖢 Γ \mathsf{C}_{\Gamma}  or  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma}  corresponding a left coset of  W s ^ W_{\hat{s}}  or  A s ^ A_{\hat{s}}  has a  type , which is defined to be  s ^ = S ∖ { s } \hat{s}=S\setminus\{s\} . The  type  of each face of  𝖢 Γ \mathsf{C}_{\Gamma}  or  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma}  is defined to be the subset of  S S  which is the intersection of the types of the vertices of the face. In particular, the type of each top-dimensional simplex is the empty set. 
 
 
 Note that if each  A s ^ A_{\hat{s}}  is spherical, but  A Γ A_{\Gamma}  is not-spherical, then  𝔻 Γ \mathbb{D}_{\Gamma}  is isomorphic to the barycentric subdivision of  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma} . When  A Γ A_{\Gamma}  is spherical, then sometimes  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma}  is also called  spherical Deligne complex  - indeed, let  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  be the reflection arrangement associated with the spherical Artin group  A Γ A_{\Gamma} . Then the spherical Deligne complex  𝕊 ​ 𝔻 𝒜 \mathbb{S}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}  defined in Section  2.4  is isomorphic to  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma} . 
 
 
 Theorem 3.1 . 
 
 [ GP12b , Theorem 3.1] 

If  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma}  is contractible and each  { A s ^ } s ∈ S \{A_{\hat{s}}\}_{s\in S}  satisfies the  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture, then  A S A_{S}  satisfies the  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture. 
 
 
 
 We recall the following notion from  [ Hua23 ] . 
 
 
 Definition 3.2 . 
 
 Let  A S A_{S}  be an Artin group with presentation graph  Γ \Gamma  and Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda . Let  S ′ ⊂ S S^{\prime}\subset S . The  ( S , S ′ ) (S,S^{\prime}) -relative Artin complex  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  is defined to be the induced subcomplex of the Artin complex  Δ S \Delta_{S}  of  A S A_{S}  spanned by vertices of type  s ^ \hat{s}  with  s ∈ S ′ s\in S^{\prime} . In other words, vertices of  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  correspond to left cosets of  { A s ^ } s ∈ S ′ \{A_{\hat{s}}\}_{s\in S^{\prime}} , and a collection of vertices span a simplex if the associated cosets have nonempty common intersection. 
 
 
 Let  Γ ′ \Gamma^{\prime}  and  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  be the induced subgraphs of  Γ \Gamma  and  Λ \Lambda  spanned by  S ′ S^{\prime} . Then we will also refer an  ( S , S ′ ) (S,S^{\prime}) -relative Artin complex as  ( Γ , Γ ′ ) (\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}) -relative Artin complex or  ( Λ , Λ ′ ) (\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}) -relative Artin complex, and denote it by  Δ Γ , Γ ′ \Delta_{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}}  or  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}} . 
 
 
 
 The links of vertices in relative Artin complexes can be computed via the following result from  [ Hua23 , Lemma 6.4] . 
 
 
 Lemma 3.3 . 
 
 Let  Δ \Delta  be the  ( Λ , Λ ′ ) (\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}) -relative Artin complex, and let  v ∈ Δ v\in\Delta  be a vertex of type  s ^ \hat{s}  with  s ∈ Λ ′ s\in\Lambda^{\prime} . Let  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  and  Λ s ′ \Lambda^{\prime}_{s}  be the induced subgraph of  Λ \Lambda  and  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  respectively spanned all the vertices which are not  s s .
Then the following are true. 
 
 1.  
 
 There is a type-preserving isomorphism between  lk ⁡ ( v , Δ ) \operatorname{lk}(v,\Delta)  and the  ( Λ s , Λ s ′ ) (\Lambda_{s},\Lambda^{\prime}_{s}) -relative Artin complex. 
 
 2.  
 
 Let  I s I_{s}  be the union of connected components of  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  that contain at least one component of  Λ s ′ \Lambda^{\prime}_{s} . Then  Λ s ′ ⊂ I s \Lambda^{\prime}_{s}\subset I_{s}  and there is a type-preserving isomorphism between  lk ⁡ ( v , Δ ) \operatorname{lk}(v,\Delta)  and the  ( I s , Λ s ′ ) (I_{s},\Lambda^{\prime}_{s}) -relative Artin complex. 
 
 3.  
 
 Let  { I i } i = 1 k \{I_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}  be the connected components of  I s I_{s} . Then  lk ( v , Δ ) = K 1 ∗ ⋯ ∗ K k \operatorname{lk}(v,\Delta)=K_{1}*\cdots*K_{k}  where  K i K_{i}  is the induced subcomplex of  lk ⁡ ( v , Δ ) \operatorname{lk}(v,\Delta)  spanned by vertices of type  t ^ \hat{t}  with  t ∈ I i t\in I_{i} . 
 
 
 
 
 
 Lemma 3.4 . 
 
 [ CMV20 , Lemma 4] 

If  | S | ≥ 3 |S|\geq 3 , then  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma}  is simply-connected. 
 
 
 
 Let  s : W Γ → A Γ s:W_{\Gamma}\to A_{\Gamma}  be the section of  A Γ → W Γ A_{\Gamma}\to W_{\Gamma}  described before. Then  s s  maps a coset  g ​ W s ^ gW_{\hat{s}}  inside  g ​ A s ^ gA_{\hat{s}} . This induces a simplicial embedding  s ′ : 𝖢 Γ → Δ Γ s^{\prime}:\mathsf{C}_{\Gamma}\to\Delta_{\Gamma} . The image of  s ′ s^{\prime}  and their translations under the group action  A Γ ↷ Δ Γ A_{\Gamma}\curvearrowright\Delta_{\Gamma}  are called  apartments  of  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma} . The quotient complex of  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma}  under the action of the pure Artin group is isomorphic to  𝖢 Γ \mathsf{C}_{\Gamma} . This quotient map  Δ Γ → 𝖢 Γ \Delta_{\Gamma}\to\mathsf{C}_{\Gamma}  restricts to an isomorphism on each apartment of  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma} . 
 
 
 Recall the following regarding homotopy types of relative Artin complexes. 
 
 
 Lemma 3.5 . 
 
 [ Hua23 , Lemma 7.1] 

Let  Λ 1 ⊂ Λ 2 \Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}  be two induced subgraphs of  Λ \Lambda  such that  Λ 2 ∖ Λ 1 \Lambda_{2}\setminus\Lambda_{1}  contain exactly one vertex, denoted by  s s . If  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ Λ , Λ 2 ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda_{2}})  is contractible for some (hence any) vertex  x ∈ Δ Λ , Λ 2 x\in\Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda_{2}}  of type  s ^ \hat{s} , then  Δ Λ , Λ 2 \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda_{2}}  deformation retracts onto  Δ Λ , Λ 1 \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda_{1}} . 
 
 
 
 Now we describe a procedure of converting a  n n -cycle in the Artin complex  Δ = Δ Λ \Delta=\Delta_{\Lambda}  of  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  to a concatenation of  n n  words in  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  (cf.  [ Hua23 , Definition 6.14] ). 
 
 
 Definition 3.6 . 
 
 A  chamber  in  Δ \Delta  is a top-dimensional simplex in  Δ \Delta . There is a 1-1 correspondence between chambers in  Δ \Delta  and elements in  A Λ A_{\Lambda} . Let  { x n } i = 1 4 \{x_{n}\}_{i=1}^{4}  be consecutive vertices of an  n n -cycle  ω \omega  in  Δ \Delta  and suppose  x i x_{i}  has type  a ^ i \hat{a}_{i}  with  a i ∈ Λ a_{i}\in\Lambda .
For each edge of  ω \omega , take a chamber of  Δ \Delta  containing this edge. We name these chambers by  { Θ i } i = 1 n \{\Theta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}  with  Θ 1 \Theta_{1}  containing the edge  x 1 ​ x 2 ¯ \overline{x_{1}x_{2}} . Each  Θ i \Theta_{i}  gives an element  g i ∈ A Λ g_{i}\in A_{\Lambda} . Then for  i ∈ ℤ / n ​ ℤ i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z} ,  g i = g i − 1 ​ w i g_{i}=g_{i-1}w_{i}  for  w i ∈ A a ^ i w_{i}\in A_{\hat{a}_{i}}  (recall that  A a ^ i A_{\hat{a}_{i}}  is defined to be  A S ∖ { a i } A_{S\setminus\{a_{i}\}} ). Thus  w 1 w 2 ⋯ w n = 1 w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{n}=1 .
The word  w 1 ⋯ w n w_{1}\cdots w_{n}  depends on the choice of  { Θ i } i = 1 n \{\Theta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n} . A different choice would lead to a word of form  u 1 ⋯ u n u_{1}\cdots u_{n}  such that there exist elements  q i ∈ A S ∖ { a i , a i + 1 } q_{i}\in A_{S\setminus\{a_{i},a_{i+1}\}}  such that  u i = q i − 1 − 1 ​ w i ​ q i u_{i}=q^{-1}_{i-1}w_{i}q_{i}  for  i ∈ ℤ / n ​ ℤ i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z} . In this case we will say the words  u 1 ⋯ u n u_{1}\cdots u_{n}  and  w 1 ⋯ w n w_{1}\cdots w_{n}  are equivalent. If in addition there exists a parabolic subgroup  A ′ A^{\prime}  of  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  such that  q i ∈ A S ∖ { a i , a i + 1 } ∩ A ′ q_{i}\in A_{S\setminus\{a_{i},a_{i+1}\}}\cap A^{\prime} , then we say  w 1 ⋯ w n w_{1}\cdots w_{n}  is  equivalent  to  u 1 ⋯ u n u_{1}\cdots u_{n}  in  A ′ A^{\prime} . 
 
 
 
 In the rest of this subsection, we look at the special case when  W Γ W_{\Gamma}  is a finite group. First, we know  𝖢 Γ \mathsf{C}_{\Gamma}  is homeomorphic to a sphere. More precisely, consider the canonical representation  ρ : W Γ → G ​ L ​ ( n , ℝ ) \rho:W_{\Gamma}\to GL(n,\mathbb{R})  and let  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  be the collection of all reflection hyperplanes in  ℝ n \mathbb{R}^{n} . Then elements in  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  cuts the unit sphere of  ℝ n \mathbb{R}^{n}  into a simplicial complex, which is isomorphic to  𝖢 Γ \mathsf{C}_{\Gamma} . From this, we know that  𝖢 Γ \mathsf{C}_{\Gamma}  and  Σ 𝒜 \Sigma_{\mathcal{A}}  are dual complexes of each other. This allows us to define the  type  of each face of  Σ 𝒜 \Sigma_{\mathcal{A}}  to be the type of its dual face in  𝖢 Γ \mathsf{C}_{\Gamma} . We define the  type  of a standard subcomplex of  Σ ^ 𝒜 \widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{A}}  to be the type of the associated face in  Σ 𝒜 \Sigma_{\mathcal{A}} . 
 
 
 We record the following description of the Artin complex  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma}  in terms of  Σ ^ 𝒜 \widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{A}} , which will be used later. 
 
 
 Remark 3.7 . 
 
 Let  X X  be the universal cover of  Σ ^ 𝒜 \widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{A}} . A  lift  of a standard subcomplex in  Σ ^ 𝒜 \widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{A}}  is a connected component of the inverse image of this subcomplex under the map  X → Σ ^ 𝒜 X\to\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{A}} .
Vertices of  Δ Γ \Delta_{\Gamma}  are in 1-1 correspondence with lifts standard subcomplexes of  Σ ^ 𝒜 \widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{A}}  of type  s ^ \hat{s}  for some  s ∈ S s\in S . A collection of vertices span a simplex if their associated lifts have non-trivial common intersection. 
 
 
 
 
 3.2  Posets and lattices 

 
 Let  P P  be a poset, i.e. a partially ordered set.
Let  S ⊂ P S\subset P . An  upper bound  (resp. lower bound) for  S S  is an element  x ∈ P x\in P  such that  s ≤ x s\leq x  (resp.  s ≥ x s\geq x ) for any  s ∈ S s\in S . The  join  of  S S  is an upper bound  x x  of  S S  such that  x ≤ y x\leq y  for any other upper bound  y y  of  S S . The  meet  of  S S  is a lower bound of  x x  of  S S  such that  x ≥ y x\geq y  for any other lower bound  y y  of  S S . We will write  x ∨ y x\vee y  for the join of two elements  x x  and  y y , and  x ∧ y x\wedge y  for the meet of two elements (if the join or the meet exists). A poset is  bounded  if it has a maximal element and a minimal element. We say  P P  is  lattice  if  P P  is a poset and any two elements in  P P  have a join and have a meet. 
 
 
 A chain in  P P  is any totally ordered
subset, subsets of chains are subchains and a maximal chain is one that is not a proper subchain of any other chain. A poset has rank  n n  if it is bounded, every chain is
a subchain of a maximal chain and all maximal chains have length  n n . For  a , b ∈ P a,b\in P  with  a ≤ b a\leq b , the  interval  between  a a  and  b b , denoted by  [ a , b ] [a,b] , is the collection of all elements  x x  of  P P  such that  a ≤ x a\leq x  and  x ≤ b x\leq b . The poset  P P  is  graded  if every interval in  P P  has a rank. 
 
 
 Definition 3.8 . 
 
 Let  P P  be a poset. We say  P P  is  bowtie free  if any subset  { x 1 , x 2 , y 1 , y 2 } ⊂ P \{x_{1},x_{2},y_{1},y_{2}\}\subset P  made of mutually distinct elements with  x i < y j x_{i}<y_{j}  for  i , j ∈ { 1 , 2 } i,j\in\{1,2\} , there exists  z ∈ P z\in P  such that  x i ≤ z ≤ y j x_{i}\leq z\leq y_{j}  for any  i , j ∈ { 1 , 2 } i,j\in\{1,2\} . 
 
 
 
 The interest of the bowtie free condition lies in the following observation. 
 
 
 Lemma 3.9 . 
 
 [ BM10 , Proposition 1.5] 

If  P P  is a bowtie free graded poset, then any pair of elements in  P P  with a lower bound have a join, and any pair of elements in  P P  with a upper bound have a meet. 
 
 
 Let  P P  be a bounded graded poset. Then  P P  is lattice if it is bowtie free. 
 
 
 
 Definition 3.10 . 
 
 A poset  P P  is  upward flag  if any three pairwise upper bounded elements have a upper bound. A poset is  downward flag  if any three pairwise lower bounded elements have a lower bound. A poset is  flag  if it is both upward flag and downward flag. 
 
 
 
 We say  x ∈ P x\in P  is a  maximal element  if there does not exist  y ∈ P y\in P  such that  x < y x<y . 
 
 
 Definition 3.11 . 
 
 A poset  P P  is  weakly upward flag  if for each triple  { x , y , z } \{x,y,z\}  satisfying the following two properties have a common upper bound: 
 
 1.  
 
 x , y , z x,y,z  are not  maximal  element in  P P ; 
 
 2.  
 
 each pair in  { x , y , z } \{x,y,z\}  have a upper bound in  P P  which is not maximal. 
 
 
 Similarly, we can define weakly downward flag and weakly flag for posets. 
 
 
 
 
 3.3  Simplicial complexes of type  S S 

 
 Let  S = { s 1 , s 2 , … , s n } S=\{s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{n}\} . A simplicial complex  X X  is of  type  S S  if all the maximal simplices of  X X  has dimension  n − 1 n-1  and there is a type function  Type \operatorname{Type}  from the vertex set of  X X  to  S S  such that  Type ⁡ ( x ) ≠ Type ⁡ ( y ) \operatorname{Type}(x)\neq\operatorname{Type}(y)  whenever  x x  and  y y  are adjacent vertices of  X X . This labeling induces a bijection between  S S  and the vertex set of each maximal simplex of  X X . 
 
 
 Note that if  A S ′ A_{S^{\prime}}  is an Artin group, and  S S  is a subset of the set of generators  S ′ S^{\prime} , then the relative Artin complex  Δ S ′ , S \Delta_{S^{\prime},S}  is a simplicial complex of type  S S . Though we will be interested in more general simplicial complexes of type  S S , for some  S S  not necessarily made of generators of some Artin groups. 
 
 
 Definition 3.12 . 
 
 Let  X X  be a simplicial complex of type  S S .
We put a total order on  S S , and define a relation  < <  on the vertex set  V V  of  X X  induced by this total order as follows:  x < y x<y  if  x x  and  y y  are adjacent, and  Type ⁡ ( x ) < Type ⁡ ( y ) \operatorname{Type}(x)<\operatorname{Type}(y) . 
 
 
 
 As all maximal simplices of  X X  have the same dimension, we know the following: 
 
 •  
 
 for each  x ∈ V x\in V  of type  s ^ \hat{s}  such that  s s  is not the smallest element in  S S , there exists  x ′ ∈ V x^{\prime}\in V  with  x ′ < x x^{\prime}<x ; 
 
 •  
 
 for each  x ∈ V x\in V  of type  s ^ \hat{s}  such that  s s  is not the biggest element in  S S , there exists  x ′ ∈ V x^{\prime}\in V  with  x ′ > x x^{\prime}>x . 
 
 
 
 
 Now we discuss a situation when the relation  < <  on  V V  is actually a poset. 
 
 
 Definition 3.13 . 
 
 An induced subgraph  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  of  Λ \Lambda  is  admissible  if for any vertex  x ∈ Λ ′ x\in\Lambda^{\prime} , if  x 1 , x 2 ∈ Λ ′ x_{1},x_{2}\in\Lambda^{\prime}  are in different connected components of  Λ ′ ∖ { x } \Lambda^{\prime}\setminus\{x\} , then they are in different components of  Λ ∖ { x } \Lambda\setminus\{x\} . 
 
 
 
 Lemma 3.14 . 
 
 ( [ Hua23 , Lemma 6.6] )

Suppose  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is an admissible linear subgraph of the Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda  of  A S A_{S}  and suppose the consecutive vertices of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  are  S ′ = { s i } i = 1 n S^{\prime}=\{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n} . Let  Δ \Delta  be the  ( Λ , Λ ′ ) (\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}) -relative Artin complex. Let  V V  be the vertex set of  Δ \Delta . We order  S ′ S^{\prime}  such that it is compatible with one of the two linear orders on  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime} . Then the induced relation  < <  on  V V  is a graded poset. 
 
 
 
 The following is motivated from the work of Haettel  [ Hae21 ,  Hae22 ] , work of Hirai  [ Hir21 ] , work of Charney  [ Cha04 ]  and work of Brady and McCammond  [ BM10 ] . 
 
 
 Definition 3.15 . 
 
 Suppose  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is an admissible linear subgraph of the Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda  of  A S A_{S}  with consecutive vertices of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  being  { s i } i = 1 n \{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n} . We say the  ( Λ , Λ ′ ) (\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}) -relative Artin complex  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is  bowtie free  if the poset in Lemma  3.14  is bowtie free. We  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is  flag  or  weakly flag , if the poset in Lemma  3.14  is flag or weakly flag respectively. Note that these definitions do not depend on the choice of the linear order on  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime} . 
 
 
 
 The following is a consequence of  [ Hua24 , Theorem 1.2] . 
 
 
 Theorem 3.16 . 
 
 Let  Λ \Lambda  be the Dynkin diagram of type  D n D_{n}  with vertex set  S S . Let  Λ ′ ⊂ Λ \Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Lambda  be the linear subgraph spanned by  { δ 1 , δ 2 , δ 3 } \{\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3}\}  in Figure  4 . Then  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is weakly flag. 
 
 
 
 Figure 4:  Dynkin diagram of type  D n D_{n} . 
 
 
 Theorem 3.17 . 
 
 ( [ Hua23 , Theorem 8.1] )

Suppose  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  is an irreducible spherical Artin group. Then for any linear subgraph  Λ ′ ⊂ Λ \Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Lambda ,  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is bowtie free. 
 
 
 
 This theorem is a consequence of Theorem  3.21  below, which we need later. 
 
 
 Definition 3.18 . 
 
 Let  Λ \Lambda  be a Dynkin diagram which is a tree, with its vertex set  S S . Let  Z Z  be a simplicial complex of type  S S .
Let  X X  be the 1-skeleton of  Z Z  with its vertex types as explained above. We say  Z Z  satisfies the  labeled 4-wheel condition  if for any induced 4-cycle in  X X  with consecutive vertices being  { x i } i = 1 4 \{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{4}  and their types being  { s ^ i } i = 1 4 \{\hat{s}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{4} , there exists a vertex  x ∈ X x\in X  adjacent to each of  x i x_{i}  such that the type  s ^ \hat{s}  of  x x  satisfies that  s s  is in the smallest subtree of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  containing all of  { s i } i = 1 4 \{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{4} . 
 
 
 
 The following is a small variation of  [ Hua23 , Lemma 6.13] . 
 
 
 Lemma 3.19 . 
 
 Suppose  Λ \Lambda  is a linear Dynkin diagram with its consecutive vertices being  S = { s i } i = 1 n S=\{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n} .
Let  X X  be a simplicial complex of type  S S . We endow  S S  with a linear order  s 1 < s 2 < ⋯ < s n s_{1}<s_{2}<\cdots<s_{n} . Assume the vertex set of  X X  equipped with the relation in Definition  3.12  is a poset. Then  X X  satisfies bowtie free condition if and only if it satisfies the labeled 4-wheel condition. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Suppose  X X  satisfies bowtie free condition. Take an induced 4-cycle  x 1 ​ y 1 ​ x 2 ​ y 2 x_{1}y_{1}x_{2}y_{2}  in  X X . We can not have  x 1 < y 1 < x 2 x_{1}<y_{1}<x_{2}  or  x 1 > y 1 > x 2 x_{1}>y_{1}>x_{2} , otherwise  x 1 x_{1}  is adjacent to  x 2 x_{2}  by our assumption, contradicting that we have an induced 4-cycle. Thus  { x 1 , y 1 , x 2 , y 2 } \{x_{1},y_{1},x_{2},y_{2}\}  forms a bowtie and the labeled 4-wheel condition follows immediately. Now suppose  X X  satisfies the labeled 4-wheel condition. If we have  { x 1 , y 1 , x 2 , y 2 } \{x_{1},y_{1},x_{2},y_{2}\}  satisfying  x i < y j x_{i}<y_{j}  for  1 ≤ i , j ≤ 2 1\leq i,j\leq 2 , then these 4 vertices form a 4-cycle in  X X . If  x 1 x_{1}  and  x 2 x_{2}  are comparable, or  y 1 y_{1}  and  y 2 y_{2}  are comparable, then the bowtie free condition clearly holds for  { x 1 , y 1 , x 2 , y 2 } \{x_{1},y_{1},x_{2},y_{2}\} . Now we assume  x 1 x_{1}  and  x 2 x_{2}  are not comparable, and  y 1 y_{1}  and  y 2 y_{2}  are not comparable. Then the 4-cycle is an induced 4-cycle. Suppose  x 1 x_{1}  has type  s ^ x 1 \hat{s}_{x_{1}}  for  s x 1 ∈ Λ s_{x_{1}}\in\Lambda . Similarly we define  s x 2 , s y 1 s_{x_{2}},s_{y_{1}}  and  s y 2 s_{y_{2}} . We assume without loss of generality that the segment  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  from  s x 1 s_{x_{1}}  to  s y 2 s_{y_{2}}  contain all of  { s x 1 , s x 2 , s y 1 , s y 2 } \{s_{x_{1}},s_{x_{2}},s_{y_{1}},s_{y_{2}}\} . Then the labeled 4-wheel condition implies that there is a vertex  z z  adjacent of each vertex of the 4-cycle such that  z z  has type  s ^ z \hat{s}_{z}  with  s z ∈ Λ ′ s_{z}\in\Lambda^{\prime} . Clearly  x 1 < z < y 2 x_{1}<z<y_{2} . Now we show  x 2 < z x_{2}<z . If this is not true, as  x 2 x_{2}  and  z z  are adjacent, we must have  x 2 > z x_{2}>z , then  x 1 < x 2 x_{1}<x_{2} , contradicting to the assumption that  x 1 x_{1}  and  x 2 x_{2}  are not comparable. Similarly,  z < y 1 z<y_{1} . Thus the bowtie free condition is satisfied.
∎ 
 
 
 
 The following is a consequence of Lemma  3.19  and  [ Hua23 , Proposition 6.15] . 
 
 
 Lemma 3.20 . 
 
 Suppose  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is an admissible tree subgraph of  Λ \Lambda . Then the relative Artin complex  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  satisfies the labeled 4-wheel condition if and only if for all maximal linear subgraph  Λ ′′ ⊂ Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime\prime}\subset\Lambda^{\prime} ,  Δ Λ , Λ ′′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime\prime}}  is bowtie free. 
 
 
 
 Theorem 3.21 . 
 
 ( [ Hua23 , Proposition 2.8] )

Suppose  A S A_{S}  is an irreducible spherical Artin group. Then  Δ S \Delta_{S}  satisfies the labeled 4-wheel condition. 
 
 
 
 The following is proved by Haettel in  [ Hae21 , Proposition 6.6] . 
 
 
 Theorem 3.22 . 
 
 Let  A S A_{S}  be the Artin group of type  B n B_{n} . Let  S = { s 1 , s 2 , … , s n } S=\{s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{n}\}  such that  s i s_{i}  and  s i + 1 s_{i+1}  are adjacent in the Dynkin diagram and  m s n − 1 , s n = 4 m_{s_{n-1},s_{n}}=4 . We put a total order on  S S  by  s 1 < s 2 < … < s n s_{1}<s_{2}<\ldots<s_{n} . Let the vertex set  V V  of  Δ S \Delta_{S}  be endowed with the relation  < <  induced from this partial order on  S S . Then  ( V , ≤ ) (V,\leq)  is a upward flag poset. 
 
 
 
 Lemma 3.23 . 
 
 ( [ Hua23 , Lemma 6.6] )

Suppose  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is an admissible linear subgraph of the Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda  of  A S A_{S}  and suppose the consecutive vertices of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  are  S ′ = { s i } i = 1 n S^{\prime}=\{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n} . Let  Δ \Delta  be the  ( Λ , Λ ′ ) (\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}) -relative Artin complex. Let  V V  be the vertex set of  Δ \Delta . We fix an order on  S ′ S^{\prime}  such that it is compatible with one of the two linear orders on  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime} , and endow  V V  with the induced order. Let  Λ ′′ \Lambda^{\prime\prime}  be a linear subgraph of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime} . Then the following holds. 
 
 1.  
 
 If  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is bowtie free, then  Δ Λ , Λ ′′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime\prime}}  is bowtie free. 
 
 2.  
 
 If  Λ ′′ \Lambda^{\prime\prime}  contains the largest (resp. smallest) vertex of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  and  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is upward (resp. downward) flag, then  Δ Λ , Λ ′′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime\prime}}  is upward (resp. downward) flag. 
 
 3.  
 
 Suppose  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  only have two vertices. If  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is bowtie free, then it is a graph with girth  ≥ 6 \geq 6 . If in addition  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is upward flag or downward flag, then it is a graph with girth  ≥ 8 \geq 8 . 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Assertions (1) and (2) follow from definition. Now we prove assertion (3). Suppose there is a 4-cycle in  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  with consecutive its vertices  x 1 , y 1 , x 2 , y 2 x_{1},y_{1},x_{2},y_{2} . Then up to a cyclic permutation of these vertices, we know  x i ≤ y j x_{i}\leq y_{j}  for  1 ≤ i , j ≤ 2 1\leq i,j\leq 2 . By the bowtie free condition, there exists  z ∈ P z\in P  such that  x i ≤ z ≤ y j x_{i}\leq z\leq y_{j}  for  1 ≤ i , j ≤ 2 1\leq i,j\leq 2 . If  z z  has the same type as  x 1 x_{1} , then  x 1 = x 2 x_{1}=x_{2} . If  z z  has the same type as  y 1 y_{1} , then  y 1 = y 2 y_{1}=y_{2} . Thus there are no embedded 4-cycles in  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}} . Similarly we deduce no embedded 6-cycles under the additional assumption of one side flagness.
∎ 
 
 
 
 The following was proved in  [ Hua23 ]  in the case when  X X  is an appropriate relative Artin complex, however, the same proof work in the slightly more general setting of simplicial complexes of type  S S . 
 
 
 Lemma 3.24 . 
 
 ( [ Hua23 , Lemma 6.9] )

Let  X X  be a simplicial complex of type  S S , with its vertex set  V V  endowed with the relation  < <  defined as above.
We assume that 
 
 1.  
 
 < <  is a partial order; 
 
 2.  
 
 for each  v ∈ V v\in V  of type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1}  or  s ^ n \hat{s}_{n} , the vertex set of  lk ⁡ ( v , X ) \operatorname{lk}(v,X)  with the induced order from  ( V , ≤ ) (V,\leq)  is a bowtie free poset; 
 
 3.  
 
 for any embedded 4-cycle  x 1 ​ y 1 ​ x 2 ​ y 2 x_{1}y_{1}x_{2}y_{2}  in  X X  such that  x 1 , x 2 x_{1},x_{2}  have type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1}  and  y 1 , y 2 y_{1},y_{2}  have type  s ^ n \hat{s}_{n} , there is a vertex  z ∈ V z\in V  such that  x i ≤ z ≤ y j x_{i}\leq z\leq y_{j}  for  1 ≤ i , j ≤ 2 1\leq i,j\leq 2 . 
 
 
 Then  ( V , ≤ ) (V,\leq)  is bowtie free. 
 
 
 
 
 3.4  Folded Artin complexes 

 
 We discuss a variation of Artin complexes and relative Artin complexes introduced in  [ Hua23 ] .
Let  Λ , Λ ′ \Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}  be a Dynkin diagram. A  special folding  is a surjective graph morphism  f : Λ → Λ ′ f:\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}  (i.e. it map vertices to vertices and edges to edges) such that if vertices  x x  and  y y  are adjacent in  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime} , then each vertex in  f − 1 ​ ( x ) f^{-1}(x)  is adjacent in  Λ \Lambda  to every vertex in  f − 1 ​ ( y ) f^{-1}(y) . 
 
 
 An induced subgraph  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is  f f -folded if  f f  restricted to  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is not injective. 
 
 
 Given a special folding  f : Λ → Λ ′ f:\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime} , we also use  f : S → S ′ f:S\to S^{\prime}  to denote the induced map on the vertex sets.
We define the folded Artin complex  Δ Λ , f \Delta_{\Lambda,f}  as follows. Vertices of  Δ Λ , f \Delta_{\Lambda,f}  are in 1-1 correspondence with left cosets of  A S ∖ { f − 1 ​ ( s ′ ) } A_{S\setminus\{f^{-1}(s^{\prime})\}}  in  A S A_{S} . A collection of vertices span a simplex if the intersection of the associated collection of left cosets is non-empty. By  [ GP12b , Lemma 4.7 and Proposition 4.5] ,  Δ Λ , f \Delta_{\Lambda,f}  is a flag complex. 
 
 
 One can also define relative version of folded Artin complex, i.e. for any induced subgraph  Λ ′′ ⊂ Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime\prime}\subset\Lambda^{\prime} , the associated relative folded Artin complex  Δ Λ , f , Λ ′′ \Delta_{\Lambda,f,\Lambda^{\prime\prime}}  is defined to be a simplicial complex whose vertices are in 1-1 correspondence with left cosets of  A S ∖ { f − 1 ​ ( s ′′ ) } A_{S\setminus\{f^{-1}(s^{\prime\prime})\}}  in  A S A_{S}  with  s ′′ ∈ Λ ′′ s^{\prime\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime\prime} , and simplices in  Δ Λ , f , Λ ′′ \Delta_{\Lambda,f,\Lambda^{\prime\prime}}  corresponds to non-empty intersection of associated left cosets. We label vertices of  Δ Λ , f , Λ ′′ \Delta_{\Lambda,f,\Lambda^{\prime\prime}}  by vertices in  Λ ′′ \Lambda^{\prime\prime} , i.e. the vertex corresponding to  g ​ A S ∖ { f − 1 ​ ( s ′′ ) } gA_{S\setminus\{f^{-1}(s^{\prime\prime})\}}  with  s ′′ ∈ Λ ′′ s^{\prime\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime\prime}  is defined to be of type  s ^ ′′ \hat{s}^{\prime\prime} . In particular,  Δ Λ , f , Λ ′′ \Delta_{\Lambda,f,\Lambda^{\prime\prime}}  is viewed as a simplicial complex of type  V ​ Λ ′′ V\Lambda^{\prime\prime} , where  V ​ Λ ′′ V\Lambda^{\prime\prime}  denotes the vertex set of  Λ ′′ \Lambda^{\prime\prime} . If  Λ ′′ \Lambda^{\prime\prime}  is a tree, then it makes sense to talk about labeled four wheel condition on  Δ Λ , f , Λ ′′ \Delta_{\Lambda,f,\Lambda^{\prime\prime}} . 
 
 
 Let  S ′′ = f − 1 ​ ( S ′ ) S^{\prime\prime}=f^{-1}(S^{\prime}) . Then there is a natural piecewise linear embedding 
 

 
 
 i : Δ Λ , f , Λ ′′ → Δ S , S ′′ i:\Delta_{\Lambda,f,\Lambda^{\prime\prime}}\to\Delta_{S,S^{\prime\prime}} 
 
 
 as follows. Given a vertex  x ∈ Δ Λ , f , Λ ′′ x\in\Delta_{\Lambda,f,\Lambda^{\prime\prime}}  corresponds to a coset of form  g ​ A S ∖ { f − 1 ​ ( s ′′ ) } gA_{S\setminus\{f^{-1}(s^{\prime\prime})\}}  with  s ′′ ∈ Λ ′′ s^{\prime\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime\prime} ,  i i  sends  x x  to the barycenter of the simplex of  Δ S , S ′′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime\prime}}  spanned by vertices of form  g ​ A S ∖ { s } gA_{S\setminus\{s\}}  with  s ∈ f − 1 ​ ( s ′′ ) s\in f^{-1}(s^{\prime\prime}) . Note that  i i  sends vertices inside a simplex to points inside a simplex. Thus we can extend  i i  linearly. 
 
 
 Lemma 3.25 . 
 
 [ Hua23 , Lemma 10.3] 

Let  f : Λ → Λ ′ f:\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}  be a special folding with  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  connected. Suppose  Λ ′′ ⊂ Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime\prime}\subset\Lambda^{\prime}  is an induced linear subgraph with consecutive vertices  { s i ′ } i = 1 n \{s^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n} . For simplicity we will say a vertex of  Y = Δ Λ , f , Λ ′′ Y=\Delta_{\Lambda,f,\Lambda^{\prime\prime}}  has type  i i  if it has type  s ^ i ′ \hat{s}^{\prime}_{i} . We define a relation in the vertex set  V V  of  Y Y  as follows. For two vertices  x , y ∈ Y x,y\in Y , we put  x < y x<y  if they are adjacent in  Y Y  and  Type ⁡ ( x ) < Type ⁡ ( y ) \operatorname{Type}(x)<\operatorname{Type}(y) . 
 
 
 Suppose  Λ ′′ \Lambda^{\prime\prime}  is an admissible subgraph of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime} . Then  ( V , ≤ ) (V,\leq)  is an order. 
 
 
 
 Lemma 3.26 . 
 
 [ Hua23 , Lemma 10.5] 

Suppose  f : Λ → Λ ′ f:\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}  be a special folding between two trees. If  Δ Λ \Delta_{\Lambda}  satisfies the labeled 4-wheel condition, then  Δ Λ , f \Delta_{\Lambda,f}  satisfies the labeled 4-wheel condition. 
 
 
 
 
 3.5  Two contractibility criterion for simplicial complexes 

 
 We recall two contractibility criterion for simplicial complexes by Haettel.
Throughout this subsection, we let  S = { s 1 , … , s n } S=\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\}  with a total order  s 1 < s 2 < ⋯ < s n s_{1}<s_{2}<\cdots<s_{n} . The vertex set of a simplicial complex of type  S S  is endowed with the relation induced from such total order on  S S . The following is a consequence of  [ Hae22 , Section 4.3, Theorem B]  and  [ Hae21 , Theorem 1.15] . 
 
 
 Theorem 3.27 . 
 
 [ Hae21 ,  Hae22 ] 

Let  X X  be a simplicial complex of type  S S . Assume that 
 
 1.  
 
 X X  is simply connected; 
 
 2.  
 
 the relation  < <  on the vertex set  V V  of  X X  is a partial order; 
 
 3.  
 
 for each  x ∈ V x\in V , let  V ≥ x V_{\geq x}  be the collection of vertices that is  ≥ x \geq x , then  V ≥ x V_{\geq x}  is bowtie free and upward flag; 
 
 4.  
 
 for each  x ∈ V x\in V , let  V ≤ x V_{\leq x}  be the collection of vertices that is  ≤ x \leq x , then  V ≤ x V_{\leq x}  is bowtie free and downward flag. 
 
 
 Then  X X  is contractible. 
 
 
 Moreover, let  Y Y  be a graph whose vertex set is the same as the vertex set of  X X , and two vertices  y 1 , y 2 ∈ Y y_{1},y_{2}\in Y  are adjacent if there exist vertices  z 1 ∈ X z_{1}\in X  of type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1}  and  z 2 ∈ X z_{2}\in X  of type  s ^ n \hat{s}_{n}  such that  z 1 ≤ y i ≤ z 2 z_{1}\leq y_{i}\leq z_{2}  for  i = 1 , 2 i=1,2 . Then  Y Y  is a Helly graph. 
 
 
 
 The graph  Y Y  in the above theorem is called the  thickening  of  X X . 
 
 
 We give an intuitive explanation of this theorem from our point of view for the convenient of the reader.
As an example to have in mind, we consider the case that  X X  is made of 2-dimensional flat triangles with angle  π / 4 \pi/4  at vertices of type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1}  and  s ^ 3 \hat{s}_{3} , and angle  π / 2 \pi/2  at vertices of type  s ^ 2 \hat{s}_{2} . Then condition 2 in Theorem  3.27  is equivalent to the links of vertices of type  s ^ 2 \hat{s}_{2}  are complete bipartite graphs; condition 1 is equivalent to that links of vertices of type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1}  have girth  ≥ 8 \geq 8  (see Lemma  3.23 ), hence CAT ( 1 ) (1) ; and condition 3 is equivalent to that links of vertices of type  s ^ 3 \hat{s}_{3}  have girth  ≥ 8 \geq 8 , hence CAT ( 1 ) (1) . In particular,  X X  is CAT ( 0 ) (0)  under the assumption of Theorem  3.27 . In higher dimension, one can identify top-dimensional simplices in  X X  with the orthoscheme  0 ≤ x 1 ≤ x 2 ≤ ⋯ ≤ x n ≤ 1 0\leq x_{1}\leq x_{2}\leq\cdots\leq x_{n}\leq 1  in  𝔼 n \mathbb{E}^{n} . The orthoscheme is equipped with  ℓ ∞ \ell^{\infty}  metric rather than  ℓ 2 \ell^{2}  metric, and  X X  is metrized in the way such that each top-dimensional simplex is an orthoscheme with  ℓ ∞ \ell^{\infty} -metric. Haettel showed that under the above link conditions,  X X  is an injective metric space, which is also a form of non-positive curvature, and it implies contractility. 
 
 
 Going back to the 2-dimensional case, Chepoi  [ Che00 ]  defined a  folder  in  X X  to be the union of all triangles in  X X  containing an edge between a type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1}  vertex and a type  s ^ 3 \hat{s}_{3}  vertex. While the complex  X X  is not exactly a CAT ( 0 ) (0)  square complex, it behaves like a CAT ( 0 ) (0)  square complex in the sense that the folders in  X X  can be treated as generalized squares, and they intersect each other in the same pattern as how squares in a CAT ( 0 ) (0)  square complex intersecting each other. More precisely, the collection of folders are cell-Helly in the sense defined in  [ HO21 ] . This leads to the thickening of  X X  - we span a complete graph on the vertex set of each folder, and Chepoi showed that the thickening is a Helly graph. In the setting of Theorem  3.27 , one can naturally extend the definition of folders and span a complete graph on the vertex set of each folder, which motivates the definition of thickening of  X X . Intuitively, the assumption of Theorem  3.27  will ensure the collection of folders intersect each other in a similar pattern as how cubes intersects each other in a CAT ( 0 ) (0)  cube complex - again the collection of folders are cell-Helly, and the thickening gives a Helly graph. This also gives contractibility from a combinatorial viewpoint as the flag completion of any Helly graph is contractible. The reader is also refereed to  [ CCHO14 , Theorem 6.17]  for comparison in the setting of sweakly modular graphs. 
 
 
 Now we discuss a variation of Theorem  3.27 .
Put a cyclic order  s 1 < s 2 < ⋯ s n < s 1 s_{1}<s_{2}<\cdots s_{n}<s_{1}  on  S S .
For each vertex  x x  in  X X  of type  s i s_{i} , we consider a relation  < x <_{x}  in  lk ⁡ ( x , X ) \operatorname{lk}(x,X)  as follows. We identify vertices in  lk ⁡ ( x , X ) \operatorname{lk}(x,X)  as vertices in  X X  which are adjacent to  x x . For each  s i ∈ S s_{i}\in S , this cyclic order induces an order on  S ∖ { s i } S\setminus\{s_{i}\}  by declaring  s i + 1 < ⋯ < s n < s 1 < ⋯ s i − 1 s_{i+1}<\cdots<s_{n}<s_{1}<\cdots s_{i-1} .
For  y , z ∈ lk ⁡ ( x , X ) y,z\in\operatorname{lk}(x,X) , define  y < x z y<_{x}z  if  y y  is adjacent to  z z  and  Type ⁡ ( y ) < Type ⁡ ( z ) \operatorname{Type}(y)<\operatorname{Type}(z)  in  S ∖ { s i } S\setminus\{s_{i}\} . The following is a consequence of  [ Hae22 , Section 4.2, Theorem A] . 
 
 
 Theorem 3.28 . 
 
 [ Hae21 ,  Hae22 ] 

Let  X X  be a simplicial complex of type  S S , with  S S  being a cyclically ordered set as above. Suppose the following are true. 
 
 1.  
 
 X X  is simply-connected. 
 
 2.  
 
 For each vertex  x ∈ X x\in X , the relation  < x <_{x}  on the vertex set of  lk ⁡ ( x , X ) \operatorname{lk}(x,X)  is a partial order. 
 
 3.  
 
 For each vertex  x ∈ X x\in X , the set of vertex in  lk ⁡ ( x , X ) \operatorname{lk}(x,X)  with this partial order is bowtie free. 
 
 
 Then  X X  is contractible.
Moreover, if a group  G G  acts on  X X  by type-preserving automorphisms, then  X X  can be equipped with a metric with an  G G -equivariant consistent convex geodesic bicombing  σ \sigma  such that each simplex of  X X  is  σ \sigma -convex. 
 
 
 
 Lemma 3.29 . 
 
 [ Hua23 , Lemma 4.8] 

Let  X X  be as in Theorem  3.28 , with all the three assumptions there satisfied. Then for any induced 4-cycle in the 1-skeleton of  X X , there is a vertex  x ∈ X x\in X  such that  x x  is adjacent to each vertex of this 4-cycle. 
 
 
 
 
 3.6  Subdivision of some relative Artin complexes 

 
 In this subsection we adjust the procedure of handling Euclidean buildings and certain Artin complex in Haettel’s work  [ Hae21 ]  to certain relative Artin complexes. 
 
 
 Definition 3.30 . 
 
 Suppose  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  is an Artin group whose Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda  contain induced subgraph  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  such that  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is a copy with Dynkin diagram of type  B ~ n \widetilde{B}_{n} , though possibly with different edge labeling. Let  { b i } i = 1 n \{b_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}  be vertices of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  as in Figure  5  left. 
 
 
 Let  Δ = Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta=\Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  be the associated relative Artin complex. We subdivide each edge of  Δ \Delta  connecting a vertex of type  b ^ 1 \hat{b}_{1}  and a vertex of type  b ^ 2 \hat{b}_{2} . We say the middle point of such edge is of type  m m . Cut each top dimensional simplex in  Δ \Delta  into two simplices along the codimensional 1 simplex spanned by vertices of type  m m  and  { b i } i = 3 n + 1 \{b_{i}\}_{i=3}^{n+1} . This gives a new simplicial complex, which we denoted by  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime} . Define a map  t t  from the vertex set  V ​ Δ ′ V\Delta^{\prime}  of  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  to  { 1 , 2 , … , n , n + 1 } \{1,2,\ldots,n,n+1\}  by sending vertices of type  b ^ 1 , b ^ 2 \hat{b}_{1},\hat{b}_{2}  to  1 1 , vertices of type  m m  to  2 2 , vertices of type  b ^ i \hat{b}_{i}  to  i i  for  i ≥ 3 i\geq 3 . We will then view  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  as a simplicial complex of type  S = { 1 , 2 , … , n + 1 } S=\{1,2,\ldots,n+1\} . We define a relation  < <  on  V ​ Δ ′ V\Delta^{\prime}  as follows. For  x , y ∈ V ​ Δ ′ x,y\in V\Delta^{\prime} ,  x < y x<y  if  x x  and  y y  are adjacent and  t ⁡ ( x ) < t ⁡ ( y ) t(x)<t(y) . The simplicial complex  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime} , together with the relation  < <  on its vertex set, is called the  ( b 1 , b 2 ) (b_{1},b_{2}) -subdivision of  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}} . 
 
 
 
 Figure 5:  Diagrams of type  B ~ \widetilde{B}  and  D ~ \widetilde{D} . 
 
 
 Lemma 3.31 . 
 
 Suppose  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is an admissible subgraph of  Λ \Lambda . Then the relation  < <  is a partial order. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Take a vertex  x ∈ Δ ′ x\in\Delta^{\prime} . Let  lk + ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}_{+}(x,\Delta^{\prime})  (resp.  lk − ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}_{-}(x,\Delta^{\prime}) ) be the full subcomplex of  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta^{\prime})  spanned by vertices  y y  of  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  with  t ⁡ ( y ) > t ⁡ ( x ) t(y)>t(x)  (resp.  t ⁡ ( y ) < t ⁡ ( x ) t(y)<t(x) ). The lemma follows from the claim that  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta^{\prime})  is a join of  lk + ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}_{+}(x,\Delta^{\prime})  and  lk − ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}_{-}(x,\Delta^{\prime}) . Now we prove this claim.
It is clear when  t ⁡ ( x ) = 2 t(x)=2  as in this case as  x x  is the midpoint of an edge of  Δ \Delta . When  t ⁡ ( x ) ≥ 3 t(x)\geq 3 , the assumption of admissible in this lemma and Lemma  3.3  imply the claim.
∎ 
 
 
 
 We will say the  ( b 1 , b 2 ) (b_{1},b_{2}) -subdivision  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  of  Δ \Delta  is  upward or downward flag or bowtie free , if  ( V ​ Δ ′ , < ) (V\Delta^{\prime},<)  is a poset which is upward or downward flat or bowtie free. 
 
 
 Proposition 3.32 . 
 
 Suppose  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  is an Artin group whose Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda  contain induced subgraph  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  such that  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is a copy with Dynkin diagram of type  B ~ n \widetilde{B}_{n} , though possibly with different edge labeling. Let  { b i } i = 1 n + 1 \{b_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n+1}  be vertices of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  as in Figure  5  left. Suppose  n ≥ 3 n\geq 3 .
Suppose  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is an admissible subgraph of  Λ \Lambda . 
 
 
 For  i = 1 , 2 , n + 1 i=1,2,n+1 , let  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  (resp.  Λ i ′ \Lambda^{\prime}_{i} ) be the connected component of  Λ ∖ { b i } \Lambda\setminus\{b_{i}\}  (resp.  Λ ′ ∖ { b i } \Lambda^{\prime}\setminus\{b_{i}\} ) that contains  b 3 b_{3} .
Suppose that the following holds: 
 
 1.  
 
 the  ( b 1 , b 2 ) (b_{1},b_{2}) -subdivision of  Δ Λ n + 1 , Λ n + 1 ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{n+1},\Lambda^{\prime}_{n+1}}  is bowtie free and downward flag; 
 
 2.  
 
 for  i = 1 , 2 i=1,2 , the vertex set of the relative Artin complex  Δ Λ i , Λ i ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{i},\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}} , endowed with the order induced from  b i < b 3 < b 4 < ⋯ < b n + 1 b_{i}<b_{3}<b_{4}<\cdots<b_{n+1}  is bowtie free and upward flag for  i = 1 , 2 i=1,2 . 
 
 
 Then the simplicial complex  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime} , viewed as a simplicial complex of type  S S  with  S = { 1 , … , n + 1 } S=\{1,\ldots,n+1\} , satisfies all the assumptions of Theorem  3.27 . Hence  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  is contractible. Thus  Δ \Delta  is contractible. 
 
 
 
 The proof is very similar to  [ Hae21 , Section 7] , we provide details for the convenience of the reader. 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Let  P = ( V ​ Δ ′ , ≤ ) P=(V\Delta^{\prime},\leq) , which is a poset by Lemma  3.31 . Note that  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  is simply connected by Lemma  3.4 . To verify Theorem  3.27  (3), it suffices to consider the case  t ⁡ ( x ) = 1 t(x)=1 .
Then the full subcomplex of  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  spanned by  P > x P_{>x}  is  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta^{\prime}) . However,  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) ≅ lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ) ≅ Δ Λ i , Λ i ′ \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta^{\prime})\cong\operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta)\cong\Delta_{\Lambda_{i},\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}}  by Lemma  3.3 , where  i = 1 i=1  or  2 2 . Moreover, the order of vertices in  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta^{\prime})  inherited from  P P  and the order of vertices in  Δ Λ i , Λ i ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{i},\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}}  as in Assumption 1 of the proposition, are consistent under the isomorphism. Thus  P > x P_{>x}  is bowtie free and upward flag by Assumption 2.
To verify Theorem  3.27  (4), it suffices to consider the case  t ⁡ ( x ) = n + 1 t(x)=n+1 . The full subcomplex of  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  spanned by  P < x P_{<x}  is  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta^{\prime}) . By Lemma  3.3 ,  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta^{\prime})  is order-preserving isomorphic to the  ( b 1 , b 2 ) (b_{1},b_{2}) -subdivision of  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ) ≅ Δ Λ n + 1 , Λ n + 1 ′ \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta)\cong\Delta_{\Lambda_{n+1},\Lambda^{\prime}_{n+1}} , which finishes the proof by Assumption 1.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Definition 3.33 . 
 
 Suppose  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  is an Artin group whose Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda  contain induced subgraph  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  such that  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is a copy with Dynkin diagram of type  D ~ m \widetilde{D}_{m}  with  m ≥ 4 m\geq 4 , though possibly with different edge labeling. Let  a 1 , a 2 , { b i } i = 1 n , c 1 , c 2 a_{1},a_{2},\{b_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n},c_{1},c_{2}  be vertices of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  as in Figure  5  right. 
 
 
 Let  Δ = Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta=\Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  be the associated relative Artin complex. We subdivide each edge of  Δ \Delta  connecting a vertex of type  a ^ 1 \hat{a}_{1}  (resp.  c ^ 1 \hat{c}_{1} ) and a vertex of type  a ^ 2 \hat{a}_{2}  (resp.  c ^ 2 \hat{c}_{2} ). We say the middle point of such edge is of type  a ^ \hat{a}  (resp.  c ^ \hat{c} ).
Cut each top dimensional simplex in  Δ \Delta  into four simplices whose vertex set is of type  { a i , a , b 1 , … , b n , c , c j } 1 ≤ i , j ≤ 2 \{a_{i},a,b_{1},\ldots,b_{n},c,c_{j}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq 2} . This gives a new simplicial complex, which we denoted by  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime} . Define a map  t t  from the vertex set  V ​ Δ ′ V\Delta^{\prime}  of  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  to  { 1 , … , n + 4 } \{1,\ldots,n+4\}  by sending vertices of type  a i , a , b 1 , … , b n , c , c j a_{i},a,b_{1},\ldots,b_{n},c,c_{j}  to  1 , 2 , 3 , … , n + 2 , n + 3 , n + 4 1,2,3,\ldots,n+2,n+3,n+4  respectively. We will then view  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  as a simplicial complex of type  S = { 1 , … , n + 4 } S=\{1,\ldots,n+4\} . We define a relation  < <  on  V ​ Δ ′ V\Delta^{\prime}  as follows. For  x , y ∈ V ​ Δ ′ x,y\in V\Delta^{\prime} ,  x < y x<y  if  x x  and  y y  are adjacent and  t ⁡ ( x ) < t ⁡ ( y ) t(x)<t(y) . Similar to Lemma  3.31 , we know  ( V ​ Δ ′ , ≤ ) (V\Delta^{\prime},\leq)  is a poset, under the additional assumption that  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is admissible in  Λ \Lambda . 
 
 
 
 Proposition 3.34 . 
 
 Suppose  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  is an Artin group whose Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda  contain induced subgraph  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  such that  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is a copy with Dynkin diagram of type  D ~ m \widetilde{D}_{m}  with  m ≥ 4 m\geq 4 , though possibly with different edge labeling. Let  a 1 , a 2 , { b i } i = 1 n , c 1 , c 2 a_{1},a_{2},\{b_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n},c_{1},c_{2}  be vertices of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  as in Figure  5  right. Suppose  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is an admissible subgraph of  Λ \Lambda . 
 
 
 Let  Λ a i \Lambda_{a_{i}}  (resp.  Λ a i ′ \Lambda^{\prime}_{a_{i}} ) be the connected component of  Λ ∖ { a i } \Lambda\setminus\{a_{i}\}  (resp.  Λ ′ ∖ { a i } \Lambda^{\prime}\setminus\{a_{i}\} ) that contains  { b i } i = 1 n \{b_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n} . Similarly we define  Λ c i \Lambda_{c_{i}}  and  Λ c i ′ \Lambda^{\prime}_{c_{i}} .
Suppose that the following holds: 
 
 1.  
 
 the  ( a 1 , a 2 ) (a_{1},a_{2}) -subdivision of  Δ Λ c i , Λ c i ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{c_{i}},\Lambda^{\prime}_{c_{i}}}  is bowtie free and downward flag for  i = 1 , 2 i=1,2 ; 
 
 2.  
 
 the  ( c 1 , c 2 ) (c_{1},c_{2}) -subdivision of  Δ Λ a i , Λ a i ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{a_{i}},\Lambda^{\prime}_{a_{i}}}  is bowtie free and downward flag for  i = 1 , 2 i=1,2 . 
 
 
 Then the simplicial complex  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime} , viewed as a simplicial complex of type  S S  with  S = { 1 , … , n + 4 } S=\{1,\ldots,n+4\} , satisfies all the assumptions of Theorem  3.27 . Hence  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  is contractible. Thus  Δ \Delta  is contractible. 
 
 
 
 The proof is similar to that of Proposition  3.32 , and is left to the reader. 
 
 
 
 3.7  A remark on weakly flagness 

 
 In this subsection we give a technical remark on the relation of weakly flagness and Proposition  3.32  in a special case. The reader could skip this subsection on first reading.
We refer to Section  3.6  for definitions of terms in the following proposition. 
 
 
 Proposition 3.35 . 
 
 Suppose  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  is an Artin group whose Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda  contains a star shaped induced subgraph  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  made of three edges glued at a common vertex  a a . Let the other three vertices be  b 1 , b 2 , b 3 b_{1},b_{2},b_{3} . Suppose  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is an admissible subgraph of  Λ \Lambda . 
 
 
 For  i = 1 , 2 , 3 i=1,2,3 , let  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  be the connected component of  Λ ∖ { b i } \Lambda\setminus\{b_{i}\}  that contains  a a . Let  Λ i ′ = Λ ′ ∖ { b i } \Lambda^{\prime}_{i}=\Lambda^{\prime}\setminus\{b_{i}\} .
Suppose that the following holds: 
 
 1.  
 
 for  i = 2 , 3 i=2,3 , the vertex set of the relative Artin complex  Δ Λ i , Λ i ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{i},\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}} , endowed with the order induced from  b i < a < b 1 b_{i}<a<b_{1} , is a bowtie free, upward flag poset; 
 
 2.  
 
 Δ Λ 1 , Λ 1 ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}}  is bowtie free and weakly flag. 
 
 
 Then the  ( b 2 , b 3 ) (b_{2},b_{3}) -subdivision of  Δ = Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta=\Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}} , denoted  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime} , viewed as a simplicial complex of type  S S  with  S = { 1 , 2 , 3 , 4 } S=\{1,2,3,4\} , satisfies all the assumptions of Theorem  3.27 . Hence  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  is contractible. Thus  Δ \Delta  is contractible. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Compare to the proof of Proposition  3.32 ,
it remains to justify Assumption 4 of Theorem  3.27  for  x ∈ P x\in P  with  t ⁡ ( x ) = 4 t(x)=4 . The full subcomplex  Δ x ′ \Delta^{\prime}_{x}  of  Δ ′ \Delta^{\prime}  spanned by  P < x P_{<x}  can be identified with  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta^{\prime}) . Note that  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta^{\prime})  is a subdivision of  Δ x = lk ⁡ ( x , Δ ) \Delta_{x}=\operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta) , and the latter is isomorphic to  Δ Λ 1 , Λ 1 ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}} . 
 
 
 We now verify that the no bowtie condition holds for  P < x P_{<x} , using Lemma  3.24 . For each  y ∈ Δ x ′ y\in\Delta^{\prime}_{x}  with  t ⁡ ( y ) = 1 t(y)=1 ,  lk ⁡ ( y , Δ x ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(y,\Delta^{\prime}_{x})  and  lk ⁡ ( y , Δ x ) \operatorname{lk}(y,\Delta_{x})  are isomorphic, moreover, such isomorphism preserves the order of vertices (inherited respectively from  P < X P_{<X}  and from  Δ Λ 1 , Λ 1 ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}} ). As  Δ Λ 1 , Λ 1 ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}}  is bowtie free,  lk ⁡ ( y , Δ x ) \operatorname{lk}(y,\Delta_{x})  is bowtie free, hence the same holds for  lk ⁡ ( y , Δ x ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(y,\Delta^{\prime}_{x}) . It remains to verify Assumption 3 of Lemma  3.24 .
Given  { x 1 , x 2 , y 1 , y 2 } ⊂ P < x \{x_{1},x_{2},y_{1},y_{2}\}\subset P_{<x}  with  x i < y j x_{i}<y_{j}  for  1 ≤ i , j ≤ 2 1\leq i,j\leq 2 , and assume  t ⁡ ( x 1 ) = t ⁡ ( x 2 ) = 1 t(x_{1})=t(x_{2})=1  and  t ⁡ ( y 1 ) = t ⁡ ( y 2 ) = 3 t(y_{1})=t(y_{2})=3 . Note that  y 1 y_{1}  and  y 2 y_{2}  have type  a ^ \hat{a} , and  x 1 x_{1}  and  x 2 x_{2}  have type  b ^ 2 \hat{b}_{2}  or  b ^ 3 \hat{b}_{3} .
If  Type ⁡ ( x 1 ) = Type ⁡ ( x 2 ) \operatorname{Type}(x_{1})=\operatorname{Type}(x_{2}) , then the bowtie free assumption on  Δ x \Delta_{x}  implies that either  x 1 = x 2 x_{1}=x_{2}  or  y 1 = y 2 y_{1}=y_{2} . If  Type ⁡ ( x 1 ) ≠ Type ⁡ ( x 2 ) \operatorname{Type}(x_{1})\neq\operatorname{Type}(x_{2}) , then the bowtie free assumption on  Δ x \Delta_{x}  implies that  x 1 x_{1}  and  x 2 x_{2}  are adjacent in  Δ x \Delta_{x} . Then  x 1 , x 2 , y j x_{1},x_{2},y_{j}  span a triangle in  Δ x \Delta_{x}  for  j = 1 , 2 j=1,2  as  Δ x \Delta_{x}  is flag.
Let  z ∈ Δ x ′ z\in\Delta^{\prime}_{x}  be the vertex corresponding to the midpoint of  x 1 x_{1}  and  x 2 x_{2} . Then  x i ≤ z ≤ y j x_{i}\leq z\leq y_{j}  for  1 ≤ i , j ≤ 2 1\leq i,j\leq 2  in  Δ x ′ \Delta^{\prime}_{x} , as desired. 
 
 
 Lastly we verify the downward flagness condition for  P < x P_{<x} .
Now take  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } ∈ Δ x ′ \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}\in\Delta^{\prime}_{x}  such that  x i x_{i}  and  x i + 1 x_{i+1}  has a common lower bound  y i y_{i}  for  i ∈ ℤ / 3 ​ ℤ i\in\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z} . Assume without loss of generality that  t ⁡ ( y 1 ) = t ⁡ ( y 2 ) = t ⁡ ( y 3 ) = 1 t(y_{1})=t(y_{2})=t(y_{3})=1  (if  t ⁡ ( y i ) = 2 t(y_{i})=2 , then we find  y i ′ < y i y^{\prime}_{i}<y_{i}  in  P P  and replace  y i y_{i}  by  y i ′ y^{\prime}_{i} ). Let  ω \omega  be the 6-cycle in  Δ x ′ \Delta^{\prime}_{x}  of form  x 1 ​ y 1 ​ x 2 ​ y 2 ​ x 3 ​ y 3 x_{1}y_{1}x_{2}y_{2}x_{3}y_{3} . We assume  x i ≠ x j x_{i}\neq x_{j}  for  i ≠ j i\neq j  and  y i ≠ y j y_{i}\neq y_{j}  for  i ≠ j i\neq j , otherwise the downward flagness is trivial. 
 
 
 First we consider the case that  t ⁡ ( x 1 ) = t ⁡ ( x 2 ) = t ⁡ ( x 3 ) = 3 t(x_{1})=t(x_{2})=t(x_{3})=3 . Then we can view  ω \omega  as a 6-cycle in the 1-skeleton of  Δ x \Delta_{x} . Let  P ′ P^{\prime}  be the vertex set of  Δ Γ 1 , Γ 1 ′ \Delta_{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}}  with induced order from  b 2 < a < b 3 b_{2}<a<b_{3} . 
 
 1.  
 
 If  y 1 , y 2 , y 3 y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}  are all of type  b ^ 2 \hat{b}_{2}  in  Δ x \Delta_{x} , then none of  x i x_{i}  and  y j y_{j}  are maximal in  P ′ P^{\prime} . Hence the weakly flagness of  P ′ P^{\prime}  implies that  { y 1 , y 2 , y 3 } \{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}\}  has a common upper bound, denoted  z z , in  P ′ P^{\prime} . By considering the 4-cycle spanned by  { z , y 1 , y 2 , x 1 } \{z,y_{1},y_{2},x_{1}\}  and applying the bowtie free property, we know either  x 1 x_{1}  is adjacent to  z z  in  Δ x \Delta_{x} , or these two vertices are identical. The same statement holds true if we replace  x 1 x_{1}  by  x 2 x_{2}  or  x 3 x_{3} . As  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  are pairwise distinct and they have the same type in  Δ Γ 1 , Γ 1 ′ \Delta_{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}} , we know  z z  is of type  b ^ 3 \hat{b}_{3} , and  z z  is adjacent to each of  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  in  Δ x \Delta_{x} , hence also in  Δ x ′ \Delta^{\prime}_{x} . As  t ⁡ ( z ) = 1 t(z)=1 , we know  z z  is a common lower bound for  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  in  P < x P_{<x} . 
 
 2.  
 
 If  y 1 , y 2 , y 3 y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}  are all of type  b ^ 3 \hat{b}_{3}  in  Δ x \Delta_{x} , then we can apply an identical argument as in the previous item. 
 
 3.  
 
 If two of  { y 1 , y 2 , y 3 } \{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}\}  have different types in  Δ Γ 1 , Γ 1 ′ \Delta_{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}} , then we can assume without loss of generality that  y 1 y_{1}  is of type  b ^ 2 \hat{b}_{2}  and  y 2 , y 3 y_{2},y_{3}  are of type  b ^ 3 \hat{b}_{3} . Then  y 1 y_{1}  and  y i y_{i}  are adjacent in  Δ x \Delta_{x}  for  i = 2 , 3 i=2,3 . As  x 3 x_{3}  is of type  a ^ \hat{a}  in  Δ x \Delta_{x} , by applying the bowtie free condition to  { y 1 , y 2 , y 3 , x 3 } \{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},x_{3}\} , we know  y 1 y_{1}  and  x 3 x_{3}  are adjacent in  Δ x \Delta_{x} , hence in  Δ x ′ \Delta^{\prime}_{x} . Thus  y 1 y_{1}  is a common lower bound for  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  in  P < x P_{<x} . 
 
 
 
 
 Suppose the  t t -value of exactly two of  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  is  3 3 . Assume without loss of generality that  t ⁡ ( x 1 ) = t ⁡ ( x 2 ) = 3 t(x_{1})=t(x_{2})=3  and  t ⁡ ( x 3 ) = 2 t(x_{3})=2 . Then  y 2 y_{2}  and  y 3 y_{3}  are adjacent in  Δ x \Delta_{x}  and  x 3 x_{3}  is the midpoint between  y 2 y_{2}  and  y 3 y_{3} . We assume without loss of generality that  Type ⁡ ( y 2 ) = b ^ 2 \operatorname{Type}(y_{2})=\hat{b}_{2}  and  Type ⁡ ( y 3 ) = b ^ 3 \operatorname{Type}(y_{3})=\hat{b}_{3} . If  Type ⁡ ( y 1 ) = b ^ 3 \operatorname{Type}(y_{1})=\hat{b}_{3} , then  y 2 y_{2}  is adjacent to both  y 1 y_{1}  and  y 3 y_{3}  in  Δ x \Delta_{x} .
By applying the bowtie free condition to  { y 1 , y 2 , y 3 , x 1 } \{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},x_{1}\} , we know  x 1 x_{1}  and  y 2 y_{2}  are adjacent in  Δ x \Delta_{x} . Thus  y 2 y_{2}  is a common lower bound for  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  in  P < x P_{<x} . The subcase of  Type ⁡ ( y 1 ) = b ^ 2 \operatorname{Type}(y_{1})=\hat{b}_{2}  is symmetric. 
 
 
 Suppose the  t t -value of exactly one of  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  is  3 3 . Assume without loss of generality that  t ⁡ ( x 1 ) = 3 t(x_{1})=3  and  t ⁡ ( x 2 ) = t ⁡ ( x 3 ) = 2 t(x_{2})=t(x_{3})=2 . Then  y 2 y_{2}  is adjacent to both  y 1 y_{1}  and  y 3 y_{3}  in  Δ x \Delta_{x} . If  Type ⁡ ( y 2 ) = b ^ 2 \operatorname{Type}(y_{2})=\hat{b}_{2} , then  Type ⁡ ( y 1 ) = Type ⁡ ( y 3 ) = b ^ 3 \operatorname{Type}(y_{1})=\operatorname{Type}(y_{3})=\hat{b}_{3} . We apply the bowtie free condition to  { y 1 , y 2 , y 3 , x 1 } \{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},x_{1}\}  and conclude in the same way as the previous paragraph. The subcase  Type ⁡ ( y 2 ) = b ^ 3 \operatorname{Type}(y_{2})=\hat{b}_{3}  is symmetric. 
 
 
 It remains to consider the case that  t ⁡ ( x 1 ) = t ⁡ ( x 2 ) = t ⁡ ( x 3 ) = 2 t(x_{1})=t(x_{2})=t(x_{3})=2 . This implies that  y 1 , y 2 , y 3 y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}  span a triangle in  Δ x \Delta_{x} , which is impossible. ∎ 
 
 
 
 
 
 4  One side flagness in type  F 4 F_{4} 

 
 In an unpublished preprint of Crisp-McCammond, they showed how to fill in certain types of 4-cycles in Artin complex of type  A n A_{n} , then Haettel  [ Hae21 , Proposition 6.6]  realized that this actually implies how to fill in certain types of 6-cycles in Artin complex of type  B n B_{n} , using the fact that  B n B_{n}  can be realized as the fixed subgroup of an involution automorphism of  A 2 ​ n − 1 A_{2n-1} . Parallel to this, in  [ Hua23 ]  we showed how to fill in 4-cycles in type  E 6 E_{6}  Artin complex, and this can be combined with Haettel’s argument to fill in certain types of 6-cycles in type  F 4 F_{4}  Artin complex, using that Artin group of  F 4 F_{4}  can be realized as the fixed subgroup of an involution automorphism of  E 6 E_{6} . 
 
 
 Lemma 4.1 . 
 
 Suppose  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  is a spherical Artin group with Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda  and suppose  α : A Λ → A Λ \alpha:A_{\Lambda}\to A_{\Lambda}  be an automorphism induced by an automorphism of  Λ \Lambda . Let  C C  be a left coset of a standard parabolic subgroup of  A Λ A_{\Lambda} . If  α ⁡ ( C ) = C \alpha(C)=C , then  α \alpha  fixes an element in  C C . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Let  A + A^{+}  be the positive monoid in  A Λ A_{\Lambda} . Let  ≼ ℓ \preccurlyeq_{\ell}  the prefix order on  A + A^{+}  and let  ≼ r \preccurlyeq_{r}  be the suffix order on  A + A^{+} . We define an order  ≼ \preccurlyeq  on  A + × A + A^{+}\times A^{+}  by  ( a 1 , b 1 ) ≼ ( a 2 , b 2 ) (a_{1},b_{1})\preccurlyeq(a_{2},b_{2})  if  a 1 ≼ ℓ a 2 a_{1}\preccurlyeq_{\ell}a_{2}  and  a 1 ≠ a 2 a_{1}\neq a_{2}  or  a 1 = a 2 a_{1}=a_{2}  and  b 1 ≼ r b 2 b_{1}\preccurlyeq_{r}b_{2} . Recall that each element  a ∈ A Λ a\in A_{\Lambda}  be written uniquely as  b ​ c − 1 bc^{-1}  with  b , c ∈ A + b,c\in A^{+}  and  b b  and  c c  do not have non-trivial common suffix in  A + A^{+}   [ Cha95 , Section 2] . This gives a (set theoretical) embedding  A → A + × A + A\to A^{+}\times A^{+} , hence  A A  inherits an order  ≼ R \preccurlyeq_{R}  from  A + × A + A^{+}\times A^{+} . As  α ⁡ ( A + ) = A + \alpha(A^{+})=A^{+}  and  α \alpha  respect the order  ≼ ℓ \preccurlyeq_{\ell}  and  ≼ r \preccurlyeq_{r}  on  A + A^{+} , we know  α \alpha  induces an automorphism of the poset  ( A , ≼ R ) (A,\preccurlyeq_{R}) . By  [ Alt98 , Theorem 1] , there is an element  c ∈ C c\in C  such that  c ≼ R c ′ c\preccurlyeq_{R}c^{\prime}  for any  c ′ ∈ C c^{\prime}\in C . Such  c c  is unique in  C C  by definition. Thus  α ⁡ ( c ) = c \alpha(c)=c , as desired.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Proposition 4.2 . 
 
 Let  S = { s 1 , s 2 , s 3 , s 4 } S=\{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4}\}  be consecutive vertices in the Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda  of the Artin group of type  F 4 F_{4} . Let  S ′ = { s 1 , s 2 , s 3 } S^{\prime}=\{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}\} . We consider the relative Artin complex  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}} , whose vertex set is endowed with the order inherited from  s 1 < s 2 < s 3 s_{1}<s_{2}<s_{3} . Then the vertex set of  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  forms a upward flag poset. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Let  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  be the Dynkin diagram of type  E 6 E_{6} . Let  { t i } i = 1 5 \{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{5}  be the consecutive vertices of the linear subgraph  Λ 1 ′ \Lambda^{\prime}_{1}  of length  4 4  in  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1} . Let  t t  be the vertex in  Λ ∖ Λ 1 \Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{1} . Consider the homomorphism  ϕ : A Λ → A Λ 1 \phi:A_{\Lambda}\to A_{\Lambda_{1}}  by sending  s i s_{i}  to  t i ​ t 6 − i t_{i}t_{6-i}  for  i = 1 , 2 i=1,2 ,  s 3 s_{3}  to  t 3 t_{3}  and  s 4 s_{4}  to  t t . By  [ Cri00 ] ,  ϕ \phi  is an injective homomorphism, whose image is the fixed subgroup of the automorphism  σ \sigma  of  A Λ 1 A_{\Lambda_{1}}  such that  σ ⁡ ( t i ) = t 6 − i \sigma(t_{i})=t_{6-i}  for  1 ≤ i ≤ 5 1\leq i\leq 5  and  σ ⁡ ( t ) = t \sigma(t)=t . We define  P i = A s ^ i P_{i}=A_{\hat{s}_{i}} , the subgroup of  A A  generated by all generators except  s i s_{i} , and  Q i = A t ^ i Q_{i}=A_{\hat{t}_{i}} . 
 
 
 Let  Δ = Δ S , S ′ \Delta=\Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  and  Δ 1 = Δ Λ 1 , Λ 1 ′ \Delta_{1}=\Delta_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}} . Note that  σ \sigma  induces an automorphism  σ ¯ \bar{\sigma}  of  Δ 1 \Delta_{1}  by sending  g ​ Q i gQ_{i}  to  σ ⁡ ( g ) ​ Q 6 − i \sigma(g)Q_{6-i} . The map  ϕ \phi  induced a map  Δ ( 0 ) → Δ 1 ( 0 ) \Delta^{(0)}\to\Delta^{(0)}_{1} , by sending a vertex associated with  g ​ P i gP_{i}  to a vertex associated with  ϕ ⁡ ( g ) ​ Q i \phi(g)Q_{i} .
As  ϕ ⁡ ( g ​ P i ) ⊂ ϕ ⁡ ( g ) ​ Q i \phi(gP_{i})\subset\phi(g)Q_{i} , if two cosets associated with vertices of  Δ \Delta  have nonempty intersection, then their associated cosets through  ϕ \phi  in  A Λ 1 A_{\Lambda_{1}}  have nonempty intersection. Thus  ϕ \phi  respects the order of vertices in  Δ \Delta  and  Δ 1 \Delta_{1} , hence extends to a simplicial map  ϕ ¯ : Δ → Δ 1 \bar{\phi}:\Delta\to\Delta_{1} . For any vertex  x ¯ \bar{x}  in the image of  ϕ ¯ \bar{\phi} , we have  x ¯ ≤ σ ¯ ​ ( x ¯ ) \bar{x}\leq\bar{\sigma}(\bar{x}) , as  σ \sigma  is identity on the image of  ϕ \phi . 
 
 
 Given three vertices  x 1 , x 2 , x 3 x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}  which are pairwise upper bounded. Let  x ¯ i = ϕ ¯ ​ ( x i ) \bar{x}_{i}=\bar{\phi}(x_{i}) . We claim  x ¯ i ≤ σ ¯ ​ ( x ¯ j ) \bar{x}_{i}\leq\bar{\sigma}(\bar{x}_{j})  for  1 ≤ i , j ≤ 3 1\leq i,j\leq 3 . Indeed, suppose  x i ​ j ∈ Δ x_{ij}\in\Delta  is an upper bound for  x i x_{i}  and  x j x_{j} . Then  x ¯ i ≤ x ¯ i ​ j ≤ σ ¯ ​ ( x ¯ i ​ j ) ≤ σ ¯ ​ ( x ¯ j ) \bar{x}_{i}\leq\bar{x}_{ij}\leq\bar{\sigma}(\bar{x}_{ij})\leq\bar{\sigma}(\bar{x}_{j})  as  σ ¯ \bar{\sigma}  is order-reversing. Let  x ¯ \bar{x}  be the join of  x ¯ 1 , x ¯ 2 , x ¯ 3 \bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{x}_{3}  - the existence of  x ¯ \bar{x}  follows from Theorem  3.17  and Lemma  3.9 . Then  σ ¯ ​ ( x ¯ ) \bar{\sigma}(\bar{x})  is the meet of  σ ¯ ​ ( x ¯ 1 ) , σ ¯ ​ ( x ¯ 1 ) , σ ¯ ​ ( x ¯ 1 ) \bar{\sigma}(\bar{x}_{1}),\bar{\sigma}(\bar{x}_{1}),\bar{\sigma}(\bar{x}_{1}) . Note that  x ¯ ≤ σ ¯ ​ ( x ¯ ) \bar{x}\leq\bar{\sigma}(\bar{x}) . Suppose  x ¯ = γ ​ Q i \bar{x}=\gamma Q_{i} . Then  σ ¯ ​ ( x ¯ ) = σ ⁡ ( γ ) ​ Q 6 − i \bar{\sigma}(\bar{x})=\sigma(\gamma)Q_{6-i} . As  γ ​ Q i ≤ σ ⁡ ( γ ) ​ Q 6 − i \gamma Q_{i}\leq\sigma(\gamma)Q_{6-i} , we know  i ≤ 6 − i i\leq 6-i , hence  i ≤ 3 i\leq 3 . Let  C = γ ​ Q i ∩ σ ⁡ ( γ ) ​ Q 6 − i ≠ ∅ C=\gamma Q_{i}\cap\sigma(\gamma)Q_{6-i}\neq\emptyset . As  σ \sigma  is an involution,  σ ⁡ ( C ) = C \sigma(C)=C . As intersection of standard parabolic subgroups is a standard parabolic subgroup  [ vdL83 ] , we know  C C  is a left coset of a standard parabolic subgroup. By Lemma  4.1 ,  σ ⁡ ( c ) = c \sigma(c)=c  for an element  c ∈ C c\in C . By replacing  γ \gamma  by  c c , we can assume  σ ⁡ ( γ ) = γ \sigma(\gamma)=\gamma . Thus there exists  x ∈ Δ x\in\Delta  such that  x ¯ = ϕ ¯ ​ ( x ) \bar{x}=\bar{\phi}(x) . As  ϕ ¯ \bar{\phi}  is an order-preserving embedding, we know  x x  is a common upper bound of  x 1 , x 2 , x 3 x_{1},x_{2},x_{3} .
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 5  Weakly flagness in type  A n A_{n} 

 
 The goal of this section is to prove weakly flagness for certain relative Artin complexes associated to Artin groups of type  A n A_{n} , see Theorem  5.6 . 
 
 
 5.1  A lattice theoretical lemma 

 
 Lemma 5.1 . 
 
 Suppose  X X  and  ( V , ≤ ) (V,\leq)  satisfy the assumptions of Theorem  3.27 .
Then  ( V , ≤ ) (V,\leq)  is bowtie free and flag. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 First we show  ( V , ≤ ) (V,\leq)  is bowtie free. We verify the assumptions of Lemma  3.24 . Assumption 1 is a direct consequence of Assumptions 2 and 3 of Theorem  3.27  (note that if  x x  is of type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1} , then  V ≥ ​ x V_{\geq}x  is exactly the vertex set of  lk ⁡ ( x , X ) \operatorname{lk}(x,X) ). Let  x 1 ​ y 1 ​ x 2 ​ y 2 x_{1}y_{1}x_{2}y_{2}  be a 4-cycle in  X X  as in Assumption 2 of Lemma  3.24 . Let  Y Y  be the Helly graph as defined in Theorem  3.27 .
As any Helly graph satisfies the 4-wheel condition (see e.g.  [ CCHO14 , Proposition 3.25] ), there exists  z ∈ Y z\in Y  such that  z z  is adjacent to each of  { x 1 , y 1 , x 2 , y 2 } \{x_{1},y_{1},x_{2},y_{2}\}  in  Y Y . As  x 1 , x 2 x_{1},x_{2}  are of type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1}  and  y 1 , y 2 y_{1},y_{2}  are of type  s ^ n \hat{s}_{n} , by the definition of edges in  Y Y  we know  z z  is also adjacent to each of  { x 1 , y 1 , x 2 , y 2 } \{x_{1},y_{1},x_{2},y_{2}\}  in  X X , as required. 
 
 
 Now we show  ( V , ≤ ) (V,\leq)  is downward flag. Let  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  be three pairwise distinct elements in  V V  such that  x i x_{i}  and  x i + 1 x_{i+1}  has a lower bound  y i y_{i}  for  i ∈ ℤ / 3 ​ ℤ i\in\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z} . We assume  { y 1 , y 2 , y 3 } \{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}\}  are pairwise distinct, otherwise we can clearly find a lower bound for  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\} . It follows from the assumption that we can assume  y i y_{i}  is type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1}  for  1 ≤ i ≤ 3 1\leq i\leq 3 . For simplicity, we will say  x ∈ V x\in V  is of type  i i  if it is of type  s ^ i \hat{s}_{i} . We will use a downward induction on  Type ⁡ ( x 1 ) + Type ⁡ ( x 2 ) + Type ⁡ ( x 3 ) \operatorname{Type}(x_{1})+\operatorname{Type}(x_{2})+\operatorname{Type}(x_{3}) . 
 
 
 The base case of the induction is that each of  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  is type  s ^ n \hat{s}_{n} . As  y i y_{i}  is adjacent to both  x i x_{i}  and  x i + 1 x_{i+1}  in  X X , the same is true in  Y Y . Thus  d ⁡ ( x i , x j ) ≤ 2 d(x_{i},x_{j})\leq 2  for  i ≠ j i\neq j  in  Y Y . As  x i ≠ x j x_{i}\neq x_{j}  for  i ≠ j i\neq j , it follows from the definition of  Y Y  that  d ⁡ ( x i , x j ) = 2 d(x_{i},x_{j})=2 . Thus the combinatorial balls of radius 1 centered at  x 1 , x 2 , x 3 x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}  pairwise intersect. It follows from the Helly property of combinatorial balls that there exists a vertex  z ∈ Y z\in Y  such that  z z  is adjacent to  x i x_{i}  in  Y Y  for  i = 1 , 2 , 3 i=1,2,3 . As  x i x_{i}  is of type  s ^ n \hat{s}_{n} , it follows from the definition of  Y Y  that  z z  is adjacent to each  x i x_{i}  in  X X . Thus  z z  is a common lower bound for  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\} . 
 
 
 Now we assume the downward flagness is verified for any  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  with 
 

 
 
 ∑ i = 1 3 Type ⁡ ( x i ) ≥ k . \sum_{i=1}^{3}\operatorname{Type}(x_{i})\geq k. 
 
 
 Take  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  with  ∑ i = 1 3 Type ⁡ ( x i ) = k − 1 \sum_{i=1}^{3}\operatorname{Type}(x_{i})=k-1 . By assumption, there exists  1 ≤ i ≤ 3 1\leq i\leq 3  and  x i ′ x^{\prime}_{i}  such that  x i ′ > x i x^{\prime}_{i}>x_{i} . We assume without loss of generality that  i = 1 i=1 . Then by induction,  { x 1 ′ , x 2 , x 3 } \{x^{\prime}_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}  has a common lower bound in  V V , denoted by  z ′ z^{\prime} . As  y 1 y_{1}  and  z ′ z^{\prime}  have at least one common upper bound (e.g.  x 1 ′ x^{\prime}_{1}  and  x 2 x_{2} ) and  ( V , ≤ ) (V,\leq)  is bowtie free, by Lemma  3.9 ,  y 1 , z ′ y_{1},z^{\prime}  have a join, denoted  z 1 z_{1} . Then  z 1 ≤ x 1 ′ z_{1}\leq x^{\prime}_{1}  and  z 1 ≤ x 2 z_{1}\leq x_{2} . Similarly,  y 3 y_{3}  and  z ′ z^{\prime}  have a join  z 3 z_{3}  and  z 3 ≤ x 1 ′ z_{3}\leq x^{\prime}_{1} ,  z 2 ≤ x 3 z_{2}\leq x_{3} . Note that each pair from  { z 1 , z 3 , x 1 } \{z_{1},z_{3},x_{1}\}  have a lower bound, and  { z 1 , z 3 , x 1 } ⊂ V ≤ x 1 ′ \{z_{1},z_{3},x_{1}\}\subset V_{\leq x^{\prime}_{1}} . By assumption 4 of Theorem  3.27 , there is a common lower bound  z z  for  { z 1 , z 3 , x 1 } \{z_{1},z_{3},x_{1}\} . Note that  z ≤ z 1 z\leq z_{1}  and  z 1 ≤ x 2 z_{1}\leq x_{2} , thus  z ≤ x 2 z\leq x_{2} . Similarly,  z ≤ x 3 z\leq x_{3} . Thus  z z  is a lower bound for  { x 1 , x 2 , x 3 } \{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\} , as desired. 
 
 
 The proof of upward flagness of  ( V , ≤ ) (V,\leq)  is similar.
∎ 
 
 
 
 The following is an immediate consequence of Lemma  5.1  and Theorem  3.22 . 
 
 
 Corollary 5.2 . 
 
 Suppose  Λ \Lambda  is a Dynkin diagram of type  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n}  with consecutive vertices being  { s i } i = 1 n \{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n} . Then the vertex set of  Δ Λ \Delta_{\Lambda} , endowed with the order from  s 1 < s 2 < ⋯ < s n s_{1}<s_{2}<\cdots<s_{n} , is a bowtie free and flag poset. 
 
 
 
 
 5.2  An injective simplicial complex for type  A n A_{n} 

 
 In the rest of this subsection,  Λ \Lambda  will be the Dynkin diagram of type  A n A_{n} .
Let consecutive vertices in  Λ \Lambda  be  { s 1 , … , s n } \{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\} .
Let  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  be the reflection arrangement of type  A n A_{n} . Up to a linear transformation, elements in  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  are  x i = 0 x_{i}=0  for  1 ≤ i ≤ n 1\leq i\leq n , and  x i = x j x_{i}=x_{j}  for  1 ≤ i ≠ j ≤ n 1\leq i\neq j\leq n .
Let  H H  be the hyperplane  x 1 = 0 x_{1}=0 . Then a simple calculation implies that deconing of  𝒜 \mathcal{A}  with respect to  H H  gives the affine arrangement  ℬ \mathcal{B}  in  ℝ n − 1 \mathbb{R}^{n-1}  made of  y i = 1 y_{i}=1  for  1 ≤ i ≤ n − 1 1\leq i\leq n-1 ,  y i = 0 y_{i}=0  for  1 ≤ i ≤ n − 1 1\leq i\leq n-1  and  y i = y j y_{i}=y_{j}  for  1 ≤ i ≠ j ≤ n − 1 1\leq i\neq j\leq n-1 . 
 
 
 Let  S 𝒜 S_{\mathcal{A}}  and  D ℬ D_{\mathcal{B}}  be defined in Section  2.4 . Then  S 𝒜 S_{\mathcal{A}}  is isomorphic to the Coxeter complex of the associated Coxeter group, hence each vertex of  S 𝒜 S_{\mathcal{A}}  has a type  s ^ i \hat{s}_{i}  for some  i i . The complex  D ℬ D_{\mathcal{B}}  is a unit cube subdivided into orthoschemes ( [ BM10 , Definition 4.1] ).
Note that  D ℬ D_{\mathcal{B}}  can be realized as the maximal subcomplex of  S 𝒜 S_{\mathcal{A}}  which is contained in the interior of a hemisphere bounded by  H ∩ S 𝒜 H\cap S_{\mathcal{A}} . Thus it makes sense to talk about types of vertices of  D ℬ D_{\mathcal{B}} , using this embedding  D ℬ → S 𝒜 D_{\mathcal{B}}\to S_{\mathcal{A}} . We assume without loss of generality that the vertex of  D ℬ D_{\mathcal{B}}  with coordinate  ( 0 , … , 0 ) (0,\ldots,0)  is of type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1} . Then a vertex of  D ℬ D_{\mathcal{B}}  is of type  s ^ i \hat{s}_{i}  if and only if the coordinate of this vertex has  i − 1 i-1  nonzero entries. 
 
 
 Note that  Σ ℬ \Sigma_{\mathcal{B}}  can be identified as a maximal subcomplex of  Σ 𝒜 \Sigma_{\mathcal{A}}  contained in one side of  H H . This embedding  Σ ℬ → Σ 𝒜 \Sigma_{\mathcal{B}}\to\Sigma_{\mathcal{A}}  is dual to  D ℬ → S 𝒜 D_{\mathcal{B}}\to S_{\mathcal{A}} . We record the following consequence of Lemma  2.13 . 
 
 
 Lemma 5.3 . 
 
 The embedding  D ℬ → S 𝒜 D_{\mathcal{B}}\to S_{\mathcal{A}}  induces an embedding  Σ ^ ℬ → Σ ^ 𝒜 \widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{B}}\to\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{A}}  which is  π 1 \pi_{1} -injective. 
 
 
 
 Let  𝔻 ℬ \mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{B}}  and  𝕊 ​ 𝔻 𝒜 \mathbb{S}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}  be the Falk complex and spherical Deligne complex defined in Section  2.4 .
A vertex of  𝔻 ℬ \mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{B}}  is of type  s ^ i \hat{s}_{i}  if it maps to a vertex of type  s ^ i \hat{s}_{i}  under  𝔻 ℬ → D ℬ \mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{B}}\to D_{\mathcal{B}} . Note that  𝔻 ℬ \mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{B}}  is a simplicial complex of type  S = { s 1 , … , s n } S=\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\} . 
 
 
 Proposition 5.4 . 
 
 The vertex set of  𝔻 ℬ \mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{B}} , endowed with the relation  < <  induced from  s 1 < s 2 < ⋯ < s n s_{1}<s_{2}<\cdots<s_{n} , is a poset satisfying all the assumptions of Theorem  3.27 . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Note that  𝔻 ℬ \mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{B}}  is isomorphic to the inverse image of  D ℬ D_{\mathcal{B}}  (viewed as a subcomplex of  S 𝒜 S_{\mathcal{A}} ) under the map  𝕊 ​ 𝔻 𝒜 → S 𝒜 \mathbb{S}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}\to S_{\mathcal{A}} . Since the vertex set of  𝕊 ​ 𝔻 𝒜 \mathbb{S}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}  (which is the Artin complex of  A n A_{n} ) with the induced order is a poset, so is the vertex set of  𝔻 ℬ \mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{B}} . 
 
 
 As each vertex of  𝔻 ℬ \mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{B}}  is lower bounded by a vertex of type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1}  and upper bounded by a vertex of type  s ^ n \hat{s}_{n} , it suffices to verify Theorem  3.27  (3) for type  s ^ 1 \hat{s}_{1}  vertices and Theorem  3.27  (4) for type  s ^ n \hat{s}_{n}  vertices. Take a vertex  x x  of  𝔻 ℬ \mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{B}}  of type  s 1 s_{1} . Then  x x  maps to  x ¯ = ( 0 , 0 , … , 0 ) \bar{x}=(0,0,\ldots,0)  under  𝔻 ℬ → D ℬ \mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{B}}\to D_{\mathcal{B}} . Note that the local arrangement of  ℬ \mathcal{B}  at  c ¯ \bar{c}  (as defined in Section  2.4 ) is an arrangement of type  A n − 1 A_{n-1} . By Lemma  5.5  below and Lemma  2.12 ,  lk ⁡ ( x , 𝔻 ℬ ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{B}})  is type isomorphic to an Artin complex of type  B n − 1 B_{n-1} , and we are done by Theorem  3.22 . The verification of Theorem  3.27  (4) for type  s ^ n \hat{s}_{n}  vertices is similar.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Now we consider the Artin group of type  B n B_{n}  with consecutive vertices in the Dynkin diagram being  { s 1 , … , s n } \{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\}  and the edge between  s n − 1 s_{n-1}  and  s n s_{n}  is labeled by  4 4 .
Consider the reflection arrangement  𝒜 ′ \mathcal{A}^{\prime}  of type  B n B_{n}  made of the following hyperplanes:  x i = 0 x_{i}=0  for  1 ≤ i ≤ n 1\leq i\leq n , and  x i ± x j = 0 x_{i}\pm x_{j}=0  for  1 ≤ i ≠ j ≤ n 1\leq i\neq j\leq n . Then vertices of the complexes  S 𝒜 ′ S_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}  and  𝕊 ​ 𝔻 𝒜 ′ \mathbb{S}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}  (cf. Section  2.4 ) are labeled by  s ^ i \hat{s}_{i}  for  1 ≤ i ≤ n 1\leq i\leq n . 
 
 
 Lemma 5.5 .
