 if consecutive vertices of  ω \omega  have type  x ^ 1 , x ^ 2 , x ^ 1 , x ^ 2 \hat{x}_{1},\hat{x}_{2},\hat{x}_{1},\hat{x}_{2} , or type  x ^ 2 , x ^ 3 , x ^ 2 , x ^ 3 \hat{x}_{2},\hat{x}_{3},\hat{x}_{2},\hat{x}_{3} , then the 4-cycle is not embedded; 
 
 2.  
 
 if consecutive vertices of  ω \omega  have type  x ^ 1 , x ^ 3 , x ^ 1 , x ^ 3 \hat{x}_{1},\hat{x}_{3},\hat{x}_{1},\hat{x}_{3} , and the 4-cycle is embedded, then there is a vertex of type  x ^ 2 \hat{x}_{2}  adjacent to each vertices of  ω \omega . 
 
 
 By Lemma  3.24 , once these two properties are established, we know  Δ Λ 4 , f \Delta_{\Lambda_{4},f}  is bowtie free. If  ω \omega  is of type  x ^ 1 , x ^ 2 , x ^ 1 , x ^ 2 \hat{x}_{1},\hat{x}_{2},\hat{x}_{1},\hat{x}_{2} , then  ι ⁡ ( ω ) \iota(\omega)  gives a 4-cycle  ω ′ \omega^{\prime}  in  Δ Λ 4 , f ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{4},f^{\prime}}  by replacing points in  ι ⁡ ( ω ) \iota(\omega)  which are the midpoint of an edge by the vertex of type  x ^ 2 \hat{x}_{2}  in that edge. Then  ω \omega  is embedded if and only if  ω ′ \omega^{\prime}  is embedded. If  ω ′ \omega^{\prime}  is embedded, it must be induced as two vertices in  Δ Λ 4 , f ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{4},f^{\prime}}  are not adjacent. Thus Lemma  3.29  implies that there is a vertex  w ′ w^{\prime}  of  Δ Λ f ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{f}^{\prime}}  which is adjacent to each vertex of  ω ′ \omega^{\prime} . Thus  ω ′ \omega^{\prime}  is an embedded 4-cycle in  lk ⁡ ( w ′ , Δ Λ 4 , f ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(w^{\prime},\Delta_{\Lambda_{4},f^{\prime}}) , which contradicts the induction hypothesis that  lk ⁡ ( w ′ , Δ Λ 4 , f ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(w^{\prime},\Delta_{\Lambda_{4},f^{\prime}})  is bowtie free. Thus  ω \omega  is not embedded. The case  ω \omega  is of type  x ^ 2 , x ^ 3 , x ^ 2 , x ^ 3 \hat{x}_{2},\hat{x}_{3},\hat{x}_{2},\hat{x}_{3}  can be treated similarly. Now we assume  ω \omega  is of type  x ^ 1 , x ^ 3 , x ^ 1 , x ^ 3 \hat{x}_{1},\hat{x}_{3},\hat{x}_{1},\hat{x}_{3}  and  ω \omega  is embedded. Then  ι ⁡ ( ω ) \iota(\omega)  is an embedded 4-cycle in  Δ Λ 4 , f ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{4},f^{\prime}} , which must be induced. Lemma  3.29  implies that there is a vertex  w ′ w^{\prime}  of  Δ Λ 4 , f ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{4},f^{\prime}}  which is adjacent to each vertex of  ι ⁡ ( ω ) \iota(\omega) . Then  w ′ w^{\prime}  is of type  x ^ 2 \hat{x}_{2}  or  x ^ 4 \hat{x}_{4} . We assume without loss of generality that  w ′ w^{\prime}  of type  x ^ 2 \hat{x}_{2} . Then  ι ⁡ ( ω ) \iota(\omega)  is an induced 4-cycle in  lk ⁡ ( w ′ , Δ Λ 4 , f ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(w^{\prime},\Delta_{\Lambda_{4},f^{\prime}}) . As  lk ⁡ ( w ′ , Δ Λ 4 , f ′ ) \operatorname{lk}(w^{\prime},\Delta_{\Lambda_{4},f^{\prime}})  is bowtie free, there is a vertex  w ′′ ∈ lk ⁡ ( w ′ , Δ Λ 4 , f ′ ) w^{\prime\prime}\in\operatorname{lk}(w^{\prime},\Delta_{\Lambda_{4},f^{\prime}})  adjacent to each vertex of  ι ⁡ ( ω ) \iota(\omega) . Let  u u  be the vertex in  Δ Λ 4 , f \Delta_{\Lambda_{4},f}  such that  ι ⁡ ( u ) \iota(u)  is the barycenter of the edge  w ′ ​ w ′′ ¯ \overline{w^{\prime}w^{\prime\prime}} . Then  u u  is adjacent to each vertex in  ω \omega , as desired.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Lemma 10.7 . 
 
 Suppose  Λ \Lambda  is a Dynkin diagram which is a complete bipartite graph, and suppose  Λ \Lambda  has  ≥ 5 \geq 5  vertices if  Λ \Lambda  is a star. Suppose each tripod subgraph of  Λ \Lambda  satisfies the conclusion of Proposition  10.2 . Then  Δ Λ \Delta_{\Lambda}  is contractible. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Suppose  Λ = Λ m , n \Lambda=\Lambda_{m,n}  which is the join of  m m  vertices and  n n  vertices. The case that one of  m m  and  n n  is 1 follows from Lemma  10.5 . Now we assume  m > 1 m>1  and  n > 1 n>1 . Let  f : Λ → C f:\Lambda\to C  be as in Lemma  10.6 . We claim whenever  f f  is a composition of two special foldings  f ′ : Λ → Λ ′ f^{\prime}:\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}  and  f ′′ : Λ ′ → C f^{\prime\prime}:\Lambda^{\prime}\to C  with  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  being complete bipartite, then  Δ Λ , f ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,f^{\prime}}  is contractible. The lemma follows from this claim by taking  f ′′ f^{\prime\prime}  to be identity. 
 
 
 It remains to prove the claim. We induct on  m + n m+n .
Consider a sequence of special foldings: 
 

 
 
 Λ → f 1 Λ 1 → f 2 Λ 2 → f 3 ⋯ → f n Λ n = C \Lambda\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{1}}}{{\to}}\Lambda_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{2}}}{{\to}}\Lambda_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{3}}}{{\to}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{n}}}{{\to}}\Lambda_{n}=C 
 
 
 such that  f = f 1 ∘ f 2 ∘ ⋯ ∘ f n f=f_{1}\circ f_{2}\circ\cdots\circ f_{n} , each  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  is complete bipartite and  Λ i + 1 \Lambda_{i+1}  has exactly one vertex less than  Λ i \Lambda_{i} . Let  g i = f 1 ∘ ⋯ ∘ f i g_{i}=f_{1}\circ\cdots\circ f_{i}  and  Δ i = Δ Λ , g i \Delta_{i}=\Delta_{\Lambda,g_{i}} . The sequence can be arranged such that  Δ Λ , f ′ = Δ i \Delta_{\Lambda,f^{\prime}}=\Delta_{i}  for some  i i . So it suffices to show  Δ i \Delta_{i}  is contractible for any  i i . We set  Δ 0 = Δ Λ \Delta_{0}=\Delta_{\Lambda} . 
 
 
 For each  i i , let  v i ∈ Λ i v_{i}\in\Lambda_{i}  be the unique vertex such that  f i − 1 ​ ( v i ) f^{-1}_{i}(v_{i})  has more than one vertices. For each edge in  Δ i \Delta_{i}  whose vertices are of type  f i + 1 − 1 ​ ( v i + 1 ) f^{-1}_{i+1}(v_{i+1}) , we add a new vertex in this edge which is the midpoint of this edge, and say this new vertex has type  m ^ i \hat{m}_{i} . Cut each top dimensional simplex in  Δ i \Delta_{i}  along the new vertex into two simplices, and let the resulting complex by  Δ i ′ \Delta^{\prime}_{i} . Then there is a natural embedding  ι i + 1 : Δ i + 1 → Δ i \iota_{i+1}:\Delta_{i+1}\to\Delta_{i}  mapping vertices of type  v ^ \hat{v}  to vertices of type  f ^ i + 1 − 1 ​ ( v ) \hat{f}^{-1}_{i+1}(v)  for  v ∈ Λ i + 1 ∖ { v i + 1 } v\in\Lambda_{i+1}\setminus\{v_{i+1}\} , and mapping vertices of type  v ^ i + 1 \hat{v}_{i+1}  to vertices of type  m ^ i \hat{m}_{i} . The image of  ι i + 1 \iota_{i+1}  is the full subcomplex of  Δ i \Delta_{i}  spanned by vertices whose types are either  m ^ i \hat{m}_{i}  or inside  f i + 1 − 1 ​ ( Λ i + 1 ∖ { v i + 1 } ) f^{-1}_{i+1}(\Lambda_{i+1}\setminus\{v_{i+1}\}) . 
 
 
 By Lemma  10.6 , Theorem  3.28  and  [ Hua23 , Lemma 10.3] , we know  Δ n \Delta_{n}  is contractible. Next we will show  Δ i \Delta_{i}  and  Δ i + 1 \Delta_{i+1}  are homotopic equivalent for  0 ≤ i ≤ n − 1 0\leq i\leq n-1 . By the description of how  Δ i + 1 \Delta_{i+1}  sits as a subcomplex of  Δ i ′ \Delta^{\prime}_{i}  via  ι i + 1 \iota_{i+1}  in the previous paragraph, it suffices to show for any  v ∈ f i + 1 − 1 ​ ( v i + 1 ) v\in f^{-1}_{i+1}(v_{i+1})  and any vertex  x ∈ Δ i x\in\Delta_{i}  of type  v ^ \hat{v} ,  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ i ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta_{i})  is contractible, as this would imply  Δ i ′ \Delta^{\prime}_{i}  deformation retracts onto  Δ i + 1 \Delta_{i+1} . As  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  is complete bipartite,  f i + 1 − 1 ​ ( v i + 1 ) f^{-1}_{i+1}(v_{i+1})  is contained in a join factor of  Λ i ′ \Lambda^{\prime}_{i}  of  Λ i \Lambda_{i} . As  f i + 1 − 1 ​ ( v i + 1 ) f^{-1}_{i+1}(v_{i+1})  has two elements, we know  Λ i ∖ { v } \Lambda_{i}\setminus\{v\}  is connected.
Let  Λ i , v \Lambda_{i,v}  be the unique component of  Λ i ∖ { v } \Lambda_{i}\setminus\{v\} . Then  Λ i , v \Lambda_{i,v}  is also complete bipartite. Let  Θ = g i − 1 ​ ( Λ i , v ) \Theta=g^{-1}_{i}(\Lambda_{i,v}) .
By  [ Hua23 , Lemma 10.4] , 
 

 
 
 lk ⁡ ( x , Δ i ) ≅ Δ Θ , g i . \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta_{i})\cong\Delta_{\Theta,g_{i}}. 
 
 
 By choice of  v v , we know  f i ​ ( Λ i ) = f i ​ ( Λ i , v ) f_{i}(\Lambda_{i})=f_{i}(\Lambda_{i,v}) . Thus  f i + 1 ∘ ⋯ ∘ f n f_{i+1}\circ\cdots\circ f_{n}  still maps  Λ i , v \Lambda_{i,v}  onto  C C .
As  Θ \Theta  is a strictly smaller complete bipartite graph compared to  Λ \Lambda , by induction assumption, we know  Δ Θ , g i \Delta_{\Theta,g_{i}}  is contractible. Hence  lk ⁡ ( x , Δ i ) \operatorname{lk}(x,\Delta_{i})  is contractible, as desired.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Theorem 10.8 . 
 
 Let  Λ \Lambda  be a complete bipartite Dynkin diagram. Then  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  satisfies the  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Note that when  Λ \Lambda  has  ≤ 4 \leq 4  vertices,  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  satisfies  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture by Theorem  9.2 . Now the theorem follows by induction on the number of vertices in  Λ \Lambda , using Proposition  10.2 , Lemma  10.7  and Theorem  3.1 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 Corollary 10.9 . 
 
 Let  Λ \Lambda  be a tree Dynkin diagram with a collection of open edges  E E  with label  ≥ 6 \geq 6  such that each component of  Λ ∖ ( ∪ e ∈ E { e } ) \Lambda\setminus(\cup_{e\in E}\{e\})  is either spherical or a star. Then  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  satisfies the  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 By Theorem  1.1  and  [ Hua23 , Proposition 9.12] , it suffices to show  Δ Λ \Delta_{\Lambda}  satisfies labeled 4-wheel condition whenever  Λ \Lambda  is a star. Take a maximal linear subgraph  Λ ′ ⊂ Λ \Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Lambda . Then  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is bowtie free - the case when  Λ \Lambda  has  ≤ 3 \leq 3  vertices follows from Theorem  3.17  and Lemma  10.3 , the case when  Λ \Lambda  has 4 vertices follows from Proposition  10.2  and the case when  Λ \Lambda  has  ≥ 5 \geq 5  vertices follows from Lemma  10.5 . Then Lemma  3.20  implies that  Δ Λ \Delta_{\Lambda}  satisfies labeled 4-wheel condition.
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 11  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1)  for some higher-dimensional families 

 
 Proposition 11.1 . 
 
 Let  Λ \Lambda  be a connected Dynkin diagram with an induced sub-diagram  Λ ′ ⊂ Λ \Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Lambda  such that 
 
 1.  
 
 Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is the Dynkin diagram of a 3-dimensional irreducible affine Coxeter group; 
 
 2.  
 
 for any vertex  s ∈ Λ ′ s\in\Lambda^{\prime} , each component of  Λ ∖ { s } \Lambda\setminus\{s\}  is either spherical, or has type in  { A ~ 3 , B ~ 3 , C ~ n } \{\widetilde{A}_{3},\widetilde{B}_{3},\widetilde{C}_{n}\} . 
 
 
 Then  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  satisfies the  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Let  𝒞 \mathcal{C}  be the class of Dynkin diagrams satisfying the assumptions of Proposition  11.1 . Let  Λ 0 \Lambda_{0}  be a 5-cycle with its consecutive edges labeled by  { 3 , 3 , 4 , 3 , 4 } \{3,3,4,3,4\} .
Let  𝒞 T \mathcal{C}_{T}  be the subclass made of members of  𝒞 \mathcal{C}  that are trees. Let  𝒞 ′ \mathcal{C}^{\prime}  be the collection of all  Λ ∈ 𝒞 \Lambda\in\mathcal{C}  such that there exists  Λ ′ ⊂ Λ \Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Lambda  of type  A ~ 3 \widetilde{A}_{3}  with Assumption 2 satisfied. We first show that element of  𝒞 = 𝒞 T ⊔ 𝒞 ′ ⊔ { Λ 0 } \mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{T}\sqcup\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\sqcup\{\Lambda_{0}\} . Indeed, take  Λ ∈ 𝒞 \Lambda\in\mathcal{C}  and let  Λ ′ ⊂ Λ \Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Lambda  be as in Assumption 2. First we consider the case  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  has type  C ~ 3 \widetilde{C}_{3} . Let  { s i } i = 1 4 \{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{4}  be consecutive vertices in  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime} .
By our assumption and the classification of spherical and Euclidean Dynkin diagrams, for  i = 1 , 4 i=1,4 , the only possibility types for each component of  Λ ∖ { s i } \Lambda\setminus\{s_{i}\}  are  { B n , F 4 , B ~ 3 , C ~ n } \{B_{n},F_{4},\widetilde{B}_{3},\widetilde{C}_{n}\} . If  Λ \Lambda  is not a tree, then it must contain an embedded cycle  C C . As each component of  Λ ∖ { s i } \Lambda\setminus\{s_{i}\}  is a tree for  i = 1 , 4 i=1,4 , we know  { s 1 , s 4 } ⊂ C \{s_{1},s_{4}\}\subset C . Note that  s 2 ∈ C s_{2}\in C , otherwise  C ∪ s 3 ​ s 4 ¯ C\cup\overline{s_{3}s_{4}}  is contained in a component of  Λ ∖ { s 2 } \Lambda\setminus\{s_{2}\} , which is not possible by our assumption (as the label of  s 3 ​ s 4 ¯ \overline{s_{3}s_{4}}  is  4 4 ). Similarly  s 3 ∈ C s_{3}\in C . Thus  Λ ′ ⊂ C \Lambda^{\prime}\subset C . Let  e i e_{i}  be the edge of  C C  that is outside  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  and contains  s i s_{i}  for  i = 1 , 4 i=1,4 .
As  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is an induced subgraph of  Λ \Lambda ,  s 1 ∉ e 4 s_{1}\notin e_{4}  and  s 4 ∉ e 1 s_{4}\notin e_{1} . Thus  e 1 ∪ s 1 ​ s 2 ¯ ∪ s 2 ​ s 3 ¯ e_{1}\cup\overline{s_{1}s_{2}}\cup\overline{s_{2}s_{3}}  is contained in a component of  Λ ∖ { s 4 } \Lambda\setminus\{s_{4}\} , and by our assumption, the only possibility of this component is  F 4 F_{4} . Similarly, the component of  Λ ∖ { s 1 } \Lambda\setminus\{s_{1}\}  containing  s 2 ​ s 3 ¯ ∪ s 3 ​ s 4 ¯ ∪ e 4 \overline{s_{2}s_{3}}\cup\overline{s_{3}s_{4}}\cup e_{4}  is of type  F 4 F_{4} . It follows from Assumption 2 of the proposition that  Λ = Λ 0 \Lambda=\Lambda_{0} .
Now we consider the case that  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  has type  B ~ 3 \widetilde{B}_{3} . Let  a a  be the center vertex and  { b i } i = 1 3 \{b_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}  be leave vertices of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  with  m a , b 1 = 4 m_{a,b_{1}}=4 . For  1 ≤ i ≤ 3 1\leq i\leq 3 , let  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  be the component of  Λ ∖ { b i } \Lambda\setminus\{b_{i}\}  containing  a a . Then for  i = 2 , 3 i=2,3 ,  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  contains an edge labeled by 4, hence is a tree by the classification of spherical and Euclidean Dynkin diagrams. If  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is a tree, then  Λ \Lambda  is a tree. Otherwise  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is of type  A ~ 3 \widetilde{A}_{3} . Then for  i = 2 , 3 i=2,3 ,  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  contains a path of length 3, with its edges labeled by  4 , 3 , 3 4,3,3 . Thus  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  is of type  B n B_{n}  or  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n}  for  i = 2 , 3 i=2,3 . However, only type  B n B_{n}  and  n = 4 n=4  is possible, otherwise  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is not of type  A ~ 3 \widetilde{A}_{3} . This implies that  Λ \Lambda  is obtained from a diagram of type  A ~ 3 \widetilde{A}_{3}  by adding an extra edge of label  4 4 . Thus  Λ ∈ 𝒞 ′ \Lambda\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime} . 
 
 
 Next we show  𝒞 ′ \mathcal{C}^{\prime}  satisfy the assumptions of  [ Hua23 , Corollary 7.3]  with  𝒞 2 = 𝒞 ′ \mathcal{C}_{2}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime} , and  𝒞 1 \mathcal{C}_{1}  be the class containing only the diagram of type  A ~ 3 \widetilde{A}_{3} , hence each element in  𝒞 ′ \mathcal{C}^{\prime}  satisfies the  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture. Note that Assumptions  2 , 3 , 4 2,3,4  of  [ Hua23 , Corollary 7.3]  are clear. It remains to show  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is contractible. For vertex  s ∈ Λ ′ s\in\Lambda^{\prime} , Let  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  be the component of  Λ ∖ { s } \Lambda\setminus\{s\}  containing the rest of the vertices of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime} . If  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  is not a tree for some  s s , then  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  is of type  A ~ 3 \widetilde{A}_{3} . Then  Λ \Lambda  is a complete bipartite graph  K 2 , 3 K_{2,3} . By  [ Hua23 , Theorem 10.7 and Corollary 10.10] ,  Δ Λ \Delta_{\Lambda}  is contractible. Hence  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is contractible by Lemma  3.5 . Now we assume  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  is a tree for each  s s . By Theorem  3.28  and Lemma  3.3 , it suffices to show  Δ Λ s , Λ s ∩ Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{s},\Lambda_{s}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}}  is bowtie free. However, this follows from Theorem  3.17  if  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  is spherical, and Corollary  5.2  if  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  is of type  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n} . If  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  is of type  B ~ 3 \widetilde{B}_{3} , then by Theorem  3.22  and Theorem  5.6 ,  Δ Λ s \Delta_{\Lambda_{s}}  satisfies the assumptions of Proposition  3.35 . Thus by Proposition  8.1   Δ Λ s , Λ s ∩ Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{s},\Lambda_{s}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}}  is bowtie free. 
 
 
 As the  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture for Artin group with diagram  Λ 0 \Lambda_{0}  follows from  [ Hua23 , Theorem 10.9] , it remains to show diagrams in  𝒞 T \mathcal{C}_{T}  satisfies  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture.
We will show  𝒞 T \mathcal{C}_{T}  satisfy the assumptions of  [ Hua23 , Corollary 7.3]  with  𝒞 2 = 𝒞 T \mathcal{C}_{2}=\mathcal{C}_{T}  and  𝒞 1 = { C ~ 3 , B ~ 3 } \mathcal{C}_{1}=\{\widetilde{C}_{3},\widetilde{B}_{3}\} . Again Assumptions  2 , 3 , 4 2,3,4  of  [ Hua23 , Corollary 7.3]  are clear, and it suffices to show  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is contractible with  Λ ′ ∈ 𝒞 1 \Lambda^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}  and  Λ ∈ 𝒞 2 \Lambda\in\mathcal{C}_{2} . 
 
 
 Case 1:  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is of type  C ~ 3 \widetilde{C}_{3} .  Let consecutive vertices of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  be  { s i } i = 1 4 \{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{4} . Let  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  be the component of  Λ ∖ { s 1 } \Lambda\setminus\{s_{1}\}  containing  Λ ′ ∖ { s 1 } \Lambda^{\prime}\setminus\{s_{1}\} . By Assumption 2,  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is either spherical or irreducible 3-dimensional Euclidean. We claim the vertex set of  Δ Λ 1 , Λ 1 ∩ Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}} , endowed with the order induced from  s 2 < s 3 < s 4 s_{2}<s_{3}<s_{4}  is a bowtie free and upward flag poset. As  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is a tree,  Δ Λ 1 , Λ 1 ∩ Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}}  is indeed a poset. If  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is spherical, then either  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is of type  B n B_{n}  or type  F 4 F_{4} . Then  Δ Λ 1 , Λ 1 ∩ Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}}  being bowtie free and upward flag follows from Theorem  3.17 , Theorem  3.22  and Proposition  4.2 . If  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is of type  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n} , then the claim follows from Corollary  5.2 . If  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is of type  B ~ 3 \widetilde{B}_{3} , then as before we know  Δ Λ 1 \Delta_{\Lambda_{1}}  satisfies the assumptions of Proposition  3.35 , and Proposition  8.1  (1) implies that  Δ Λ 1 , Λ 1 ∩ Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}}  is bowtie free and upward flag. Thus the claim is proved. Similarly, if  Λ 4 \Lambda_{4}  is the component of  Λ ∖ { s 4 } \Lambda\setminus\{s_{4}\}  containing  Λ ′ ∖ { s 4 } \Lambda^{\prime}\setminus\{s_{4}\} , then the vertex set of  Δ Λ 4 , Λ 4 ∩ Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{4},\Lambda_{4}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}} , endowed with the order induced from  s 1 < s 2 < s 3 s_{1}<s_{2}<s_{3}  is a bowtie free and downward flag poset. As  Λ \Lambda  is a tree,  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  with its vertices endowed with the order induced from  s 1 < s 2 < s 3 < s 4 s_{1}<s_{2}<s_{3}<s_{4}  is a poset. Now Theorem  3.27  implies that  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is contractible. 
 
 
 Case 2:  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is of type  B ~ 3 \widetilde{B}_{3} .  Let  a a  be the center vertex and  { b i } i = 1 3 \{b_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}  be leave vertices of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  with  m a , b 1 = 4 m_{a,b_{1}}=4 . For  1 ≤ i ≤ 3 1\leq i\leq 3 , let  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  be the component of  Λ ∖ { b i } \Lambda\setminus\{b_{i}\}  containing  a a . Now we verify the assumptions of Proposition  3.35  hold for  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}} . As  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  contains an edge labeled  4 4  for  i = 2 , 3 i=2,3 , we know the type of  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  belongs to  { F 4 , B n , C ~ n , B ~ 3 } \{F_{4},B_{n},\widetilde{C}_{n},\widetilde{B}_{3}\} . Then Assumption 1 of Proposition  3.35  follows from Proposition  4.2  if  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  is of type  F 4 F_{4} , Proposition  3.22  if  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  is of type  B n B_{n} , Corollary  5.2  if  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  is of type  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n} , and Proposition  8.1  (1) if  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  is of type  B ~ 3 \widetilde{B}_{3} . For Assumption 2 of Proposition  3.35 , note that the type of  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  belongs to  { A n , B n , D 4 , C ~ n , B ~ 3 } \{A_{n},B_{n},D_{4},\widetilde{C}_{n},\widetilde{B}_{3}\}  (otherwise we will have a contradiction with the fact that the type of  Λ i \Lambda_{i}  belongs to  { F 4 , B n , C ~ n , B ~ 3 } \{F_{4},B_{n},\widetilde{C}_{n},\widetilde{B}_{3}\}  for  i = 2 , 3 i=2,3 ). Thus Assumption 2 of Proposition  3.35  follows from Theorem  5.6  if  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is of type  A n A_{n} , Proposition  8.1  (2) if  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is of type  B ~ 3 \widetilde{B}_{3} , Theorem  3.16  if  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is of type  D 4 D_{4} , and Lemma  11.2  below if  Λ 1 \Lambda_{1}  is of type  B n B_{n}  or  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n} .
∎ 
 
 
 
 Lemma 11.2 . 
 
 Suppose  Λ \Lambda  is a Dynkin diagram of type  B n , C ~ n , H 3 B_{n},\widetilde{C}_{n},H_{3}  or  F 4 F_{4} . Let  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  be a linear subgraph with three vertices. Then  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is weakly flag. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 First assume  Λ \Lambda  is of type  B n B_{n} . We label consecutive vertices of  Λ \Lambda  by  { s i } i = 1 n \{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}  with  m s n − 1 , s n = 4 m_{s_{n-1},s_{n}}=4 . Then vertices of  Δ Λ \Delta_{\Lambda}  with the order induced from  s 1 < s 2 < ⋯ < s n s_{1}<s_{2}<\cdots<s_{n}  is upward flag. Suppose  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  has vertices  { s i , s i + 1 , s i + 2 } \{s_{i},s_{i+1},s_{i+2}\} . Then we need to show: 
 
 1.  
 
 if  { x i } i = 1 3 \{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}  are type  s ^ i \hat{s}_{i}  elements such that  x i x_{i}  and  x i + 1 x_{i+1}  have a common upper bound  y i y_{i}  of type  s ^ i + 1 \hat{s}_{i+1}  for  i ∈ ℤ / 3 ​ ℤ i\in\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z} , then  { x i } i = 1 3 \{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}  have a common upper bound of type  s ^ i + 2 \hat{s}_{i+2} ; 
 
 2.  
 
 if  { x i } i = 1 3 \{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}  are type  s ^ i + 2 \hat{s}_{i+2}  elements such that  x i x_{i}  and  x i + 1 x_{i+1}  have a common lower bound  y i y_{i}  of type  s ^ i + 1 \hat{s}_{i+1}  for  i ∈ ℤ / 3 ​ ℤ i\in\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z} , then  { x i } i = 1 3 \{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}  have a common lower bound of type  s ^ i \hat{s}_{i} . 
 
 
 We will assume without loss of generality that  { x i } i = 1 3 \{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}  and  { y i } i = 1 3 \{y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}  are pairwise distinct.
For (1), note that the upward flagness implies that  { x i } i = 1 3 \{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}  has a common upper bound, say  z z , of type  s j s_{j} . By the pairwise distinct assumption,  j ≥ i + 2 j\geq i+2 . If  j = i + 2 j=i+2 , then we are done. If  j > i + 2 j>i+2 , let  𝒫 \mathcal{P}  be the collection of vertices in  Δ Λ \Delta_{\Lambda}  which is  < z <z . As  y i y_{i}  is the join of  x i x_{i}  and  x i + 1 x_{i+1} , we know  y i ∈ 𝒫 y_{i}\in\mathcal{P}  for all  i i . Let  Λ j \Lambda_{j}  be the subgraph of  Λ \Lambda  spanned by all  s i s_{i}  with  i < j i<j . Then  𝒫 \mathcal{P}  can be identified with the vertex set of  Δ Λ j \Delta_{\Lambda_{j}} , endowed with the induced order from  s 1 < ⋯ < s j s_{1}<\cdots<s_{j} . As  Λ j \Lambda_{j}  is of type  A j − 1 A_{j-1} , by Theorem  5.6 ,  { x i } i = 1 3 \{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}  has a common upper bound of type  s ^ i + 2 \hat{s}_{i+2} . For (2), by upward flagness,  { y i } i = 1 3 \{y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}  has a common upper bound  z z . Let  𝒫 \mathcal{P}  be the collection of vertices in  Δ Λ \Delta_{\Lambda}  which is  < z <z . Again we have  { x i } i = 1 3 ⊂ 𝒫 \{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{3}\subset\mathcal{P} , and we are done by Theorem  5.6 . The case  Λ \Lambda  is of type  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n}  is similar, using Corollary  5.2 , and the  B n B_{n}  version of Lemma  11.2 . The case of  F 4 F_{4}  or  H 3 H_{3}  is similar as well, using Proposition  4.2  and Theorem  7.1 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 Corollary 11.3 . 
 
 Suppose  Λ \Lambda  is a Dynkin diagram belonging to one of the following seven families in Figure  16 . Then  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  satisfies the  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture. 
 
 
 
 Figure 16:  Seven families. 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Let  𝒞 2 , 𝒞 3 , 𝒞 4 \mathcal{C}_{2},\mathcal{C}_{3},\mathcal{C}_{4}  be the families in first row right, second row left, and second two right respectively in Figure  16 . All families in Figure  16  except  𝒞 2 , 𝒞 3 \mathcal{C}_{2},\mathcal{C}_{3}  and  𝒞 4 \mathcal{C}_{4}  follow from Proposition  11.1  (the choice of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  are indicated in the thickened subgraphs). Let  𝒞 2 \mathcal{C}_{2}  be the remaining family of Dynkin diagrams. We will verify the assumptions of  [ Hua23 , Corollary 7.3]  with  𝒞 1 = { B ~ 3 } \mathcal{C}_{1}=\{\widetilde{B}_{3}\} . Assumptions  2 , 3 2,3  of  [ Hua23 , Corollary 7.3]  are clear.
Take  Λ ∈ 𝒞 2 \Lambda\in\mathcal{C}_{2}  and let  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  be the thickened subgraph of  Λ \Lambda . Then  Λ ∖ { s } \Lambda\setminus\{s\}  is spherical for  s = b 1 s=b_{1}  or  a a , and  Λ ∖ { s } \Lambda\setminus\{s\}  belongs to the family on the left side of second row for  s = b 2 s=b_{2}  or  b 3 b_{3} . Thus Assumption  4 4  of  [ Hua23 , Corollary 7.3]  follows. It remains to show  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is contractible. For  i = 1 , 2 i=1,2 , let  Λ i = Λ ∖ { b i } \Lambda_{i}=\Lambda\setminus\{b_{i}\} . Let  Λ i ′ \Lambda^{\prime}_{i}  be the subgraph spanned by  Λ i ∩ Λ ′ \Lambda_{i}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}  and  { b 4 } \{b_{4}\} . Then by the same argument as in the proof of Proposition  11.1 , we know  Δ Λ i , Λ i ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{i},\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}}  satisfies the assumptions of Proposition  3.35 . Thus Proposition  8.1  (1) implies that  Δ Λ i , Λ i ∩ Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{i},\Lambda_{i}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}}  is a bowtie free, upward flag poset with its vertex set endowed with the order induced from  b i < a < b 1 b_{i}<a<b_{1} . Thus Assumption 1 of Proposition  3.35  holds for  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}} . Assumption 2 also holds, by Theorem  3.16 . Thus  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is contractible by Proposition  3.35 . 
 
 
 Treating families  𝒞 3 \mathcal{C}_{3}  and  𝒞 4 \mathcal{C}_{4}  reduces to showing  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  is contractible, where  Λ \Lambda  is a diagram in  𝒞 3 \mathcal{C}_{3}  or  𝒞 4 \mathcal{C}_{4} , and  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is the thickened  A ~ 3 \widetilde{A}_{3}  subdiagram in Figure  16 . For a vertex  s ∈ Λ ′ s\in\Lambda^{\prime} , let  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  be the component of  Λ ∖ { s } \Lambda\setminus\{s\}  containing  Λ ′ ∖ { s } \Lambda^{\prime}\setminus\{s\} . By Theorem  3.27  and Lemma  3.4 , it suffices to show  Δ Λ s , λ s ∩ Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{s},\lambda_{s}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}}  is bowtie free for each  s ∈ Λ ′ s\in\Lambda^{\prime} . Note that  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  is of type  B n , D n , B ~ 4 B_{n},D_{n},\widetilde{B}_{4}  or  D ~ 4 \widetilde{D}_{4} . The  B n B_{n}  and  D n D_{n}  case follows from Theorem  3.17 .
If  Λ s \Lambda_{s}  is of type  B ~ 4 \widetilde{B}_{4} , then by Theorem  10.1  and Theorem  3.22 ,  Δ Λ s \Delta_{\Lambda_{s}}  satisfies the assumptions of Proposition  3.32 . By Proposition  8.1  (3) (the “in addition” assumption follows from Theorem  10.1 ) and Lemma  3.20 ,  Δ Λ s , Λ s ∩ Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{s},\Lambda_{s}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}}  is bowtie free. If  Λ 3 \Lambda_{3}  is of type  D ~ 4 \widetilde{D}_{4} , then by Theorem  10.1 ,  Δ Λ s \Delta_{\Lambda_{s}}  satisfies the assumptions of Proposition  3.34 . Thus by Proposition  8.8   Δ Λ s , Λ s ∩ Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda_{s},\Lambda_{s}\cap\Lambda^{\prime}}  is bowtie free.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Corollary 11.4 . 
 
 Let  W Λ W_{\Lambda}  be a reflection group acting on  ℍ n \mathbb{H}^{n}  with  n ≤ 4 n\leq 4  such that the fundamental domain is a finite volume non-compact simplex, and  Λ \Lambda  is the Dynkin diagram. Then the  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture holds for  A Λ A_{\Lambda} . 
 
 
 
 Figure 17:  Dynkin diagrams for 4-dimensional quasi-Lanner groups. 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The  n ≤ 2 n\leq 2  case follows from  [ CD95a ] . The  n = 3 n=3  case follows from Corollary  9.3 . For  n = 4 n=4 , the only possible Dynkin diagrams are shown in Figure  17 . The  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture for diagrams (1), (2) and (4) are proved in  [ Hua23 ] . The remaining diagrams are consequences of Proposition  11.1  (the choice of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is the thickened subgraphs in Figure  17 ).
∎ 
 
 
 
 Theorem 11.5 . 
 
 Suppose  Λ \Lambda  is a tree Dynkin diagram with a vertex  s ∈ Λ s\in\Lambda  such that 
 
 1.  
 
 each edge containing  s s  has label  = 3 =3 ; 
 
 2.  
 
 each component of  Λ ∖ { s } \Lambda\setminus\{s\}  either contains only one vertex, or contains only one edge labeled by  4 4 . 
 
 
 Then  A Λ A_{\Lambda}  satisfies the  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Denote the vertices of  Λ \Lambda  adjacent to  s s  by  { b i } i = 1 n \{b_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n} .
Let  e i e_{i}  be the edge labeled by  4 4  that contains  b i b_{i} , whenever such an edge exists. Let  t i t_{i}  be the valence one vertex of  e i e_{i} .
For  1 ≤ i , j , k ≤ n 1\leq i,j,k\leq n , let  Λ i ​ j ​ k \Lambda_{ijk}  be the subgraph of  Λ \Lambda  spanned by  a , b i , b j , b k a,b_{i},b_{j},b_{k} . Similarly we define subgraphs  Λ i ​ j \Lambda_{ij}  and  Λ i ​ j ​ k ​ ℓ \Lambda_{ijk\ell}  of  Λ \Lambda .
Let  Λ e i , j = Λ i ​ j ∪ e i \Lambda_{e_{i},j}=\Lambda_{ij}\cup e_{i} , whenever  e i e_{i}  exists. Let  Λ e i , e j = Λ i ​ j ∪ e i ∪ e j \Lambda_{e_{i},e_{j}}=\Lambda_{ij}\cup e_{i}\cup e_{j}  whenever  e i e_{i}  and  e j e_{j}  exist.
We claim that 
 
 1.  
 
 for any pairwise distinct  { i , j , k } \{i,j,k\} , the  ( i , j ) (i,j) -subdivision,  ( i , k ) (i,k) -subdivision and  ( j , k ) (j,k) -subdivision of  Δ Λ , Λ i ​ j ​ k \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda_{ijk}}  are all bowtie free and downward flag; 
 
 2.  
 
 the vertex set of  Δ Λ , Λ e i , j \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda_{e_{i},j}} , endowed with the order induced from the linear order in  Λ e i , j \Lambda_{e_{i},j}  with  t i t_{i}  being the largest vertex, is bowtie free and upward flag. 
 
 
 
 
 Let  m m  be the number of edges in  Λ \Lambda  labeled by  4 4 . Note that  m ≤ n m\leq n . We prove the claim by induction on  n + m n+m . The base cases are  n = 3 n=3  and  m = 0 m=0 , where Assertion 1 follows from Theorem  10.1  and Assertion 2 is empty; and  n = 2 n=2  and  m = 1 m=1 , where case Assertion 2 follows from Theorem  3.22  and Assertion 1 is empty. 
 
 
 First we show Assertion 2 of the claim. As  n ≥ 3 n\geq 3 , we can find  k ∉ { i , j } k\notin\{i,j\} . Consider  Λ ′ = e i ∪ Λ i ​ j ​ k \Lambda^{\prime}=e_{i}\cup\Lambda_{ijk} . By our induction assumption and Proposition  3.32 , the  ( b j , b k ) (b_{j},b_{k}) -subdivision of  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  satisfies all the assumptions of Theorem  3.27 . By Proposition  8.1  (1) and (2), the vertex set of  Δ Λ , Λ e i , j \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda_{e_{i},j}}  with the desired order is a bowtie free and upward flag poset, and the  ( b j , b k ) (b_{j},b_{k}) -subdivision of  Δ Λ , Λ i ​ j ​ k \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda_{ijk}}  is bowtie free and downward flag. By Proposition  8.2  and our induction assumption, the  ( b i , b j ) (b_{i},b_{j}) -subdivision and  ( b j , b k ) (b_{j},b_{k}) -subdivision of  Δ Λ , Λ i ​ j ​ k \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda_{ijk}}  are also bowtie free and downward flag. 
 
 
 Now we show Assertion 1 of the claim. If at least one of  { e i , e j , e k } \{e_{i},e_{j},e_{k}\}  exists, then Assertion 1 follows from the same argument in the previous paragraph. Now we assume none of  { e i , e j , e k } \{e_{i},e_{j},e_{k}\}  exists. If  n = 3 n=3 , then we must have  m = 0 m=0 , which is one of the base cases of the induction. Now assume  n > 3 n>3 . Then there is  ℓ ∉ { i , j , k } \ell\notin\{i,j,k\} . Now we consider  Δ Λ , Λ i ​ j ​ k ​ ℓ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda_{ijk\ell}} . By induction, all the assumptions of Proposition  3.34  are satisfied (with  n = 1 n=1  in Proposition  3.34 ). Thus Proposition  8.8  (1) and (2) imply that Assertion 1 holds for a particular subdivision of  Δ Λ , Λ i ​ j ​ k \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda_{ijk}} . Other subdivisions can be treated by symmetry. 
 
 
 Let  𝒞 2 \mathcal{C}_{2}  be the class of Dynkin diagrams satisfying the two requirements of Theorem  11.5 . Let  𝒞 1 = { B ~ 4 , C ~ 4 , D ~ 4 } \mathcal{C}_{1}=\{\widetilde{B}_{4},\widetilde{C}_{4},\widetilde{D}_{4}\} . We now verify all the assumptions of  [ Hua23 , Proposition 7.2]  hold. Assumption 1 of  [ Hua23 , Proposition 7.2]  follows from the above claim, Proposition  3.35 , Theorem  3.27 , and Proposition  3.34 . Assumption 2 of  [ Hua23 , Proposition 7.2]  is clear. Let  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  be as in Assumption 3 of  [ Hua23 , Proposition 7.2] . Then  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is spherical, hence satisfies  K ⁡ ( π , 1 ) K(\pi,1) -conjecture by  [ Del72 ] . Now we are done by  [ Hua23 , Proposition 7.2] .
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 Appendix A  Proposed metric on the core 

 
 We propose two metrics on the complex  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  discussed in Conjecture  1.13 : one conjectured to be CAT ( 0 ) (0) , and another conjectured to satisfy other notions of non-positive curvature  [ Lan13 ,  DL15 ,  CCHO14 ,  CCG+20 ] .
The first one is easier to define, but harder to work with (we actually do not work with it); the second one takes more effort to define, but easier to work with. 
 
 
 For an Artin group  A S A_{S} , we use  S S  to denote the standard generating set,  Γ \Gamma  to denote the presentation graph, and  Λ \Lambda  to denote the Dynkin diagram. We say the Artin group  A S A_{S}   dominates  another Artin group  A S ′ A_{S^{\prime}}  if there exists an isomorphism of their presentation graphs  f : Γ → Γ ′ f:\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}  such that the label of each edge  e ⊂ Γ e\subset\Gamma  is  ≥ \geq  the label of  f ⁡ ( e ) f(e) . The following is a consequence of  [ Lan50b ] . 
 
 
 Lemma A.1 . 
 
 Suppose  A S A_{S}  is an irreducible almost spherical type Artin group. Then 
 
 1.  
 
 either  A S A_{S}  dominates an irreducible Artin group of Euclidean type, or  A S A_{S}  has Dynkin diagram  [ 3 , 5 , 3 ] [3,5,3]  or  [ 5 , 3 , 3 , 3 ] [5,3,3,3] ; 
 
 2.  
 
 if in addition  A S A_{S}  is not of type  E ~ 6 , E ~ 7 , E ~ 8 , F ~ 4 , [ 3 , 5 , 3 ] , [ 5 , 3 , 3 , 5 ] \widetilde{E}_{6},\widetilde{E}_{7},\widetilde{E}_{8},\widetilde{F}_{4},[3,5,3],[5,3,3,5] , then  A S A_{S}  dominates an irreducible Artin group of whose type belong to  { G ~ 2 , A ~ n , B ~ n , C ~ n , D ~ n } \{\widetilde{G}_{2},\widetilde{A}_{n},\widetilde{B}_{n},\widetilde{C}_{n},\widetilde{D}_{n}\} . 
 
 
 
 
 
 Here  [ 3 , 5 , 3 ] [3,5,3]  is the linear Dynkin diagram whose consecutive edges are labeled by  3 , 5 3,5  and  3 3 . Similarly we define  [ 5 , 3 , 3 , 3 ] [5,3,3,3] . 
 
 
 Definition A.2   (Proposed metric I) . 
 
 Given an Artin group  A S A_{S} , and let  S ′ ⊂ S S^{\prime}\subset S  be an irreducible almost spherical subset. To metrize the relative Artin complex, it suffices to metrize the fundamental domain with respect to the action  A S ↷ Δ S , S ′ A_{S}\curvearrowright\Delta_{S,S^{\prime}} , which is a simplex  K S ′ K_{S^{\prime}}  whose vertices are of type  s ^ \hat{s}  with  s ∈ S ′ s\in S^{\prime} . Now we choose a shape for this fundamental domain as follows. 
 
 
 Let  A S ′′ A_{S^{\prime\prime}}  be an Artin group dominated by  A S ′ A_{S^{\prime}}  under a bijection  f : S ′ → S ′′ f:S^{\prime}\to S^{\prime\prime}  such that  A S ′′ A_{S^{\prime\prime}}  is either Euclidean, or  [ 3 , 5 , 3 ] [3,5,3] , or  [ 5 , 3 , 3 , 3 ] [5,3,3,3] . Then associated Coxeter group  W S ′′ W_{S^{\prime\prime}}  acts properly and cocompactly by isometries on a metric space  X X  with  X X  being either  𝔼 n \mathbb{E}^{n}  or  ℍ 3 \mathbb{H}^{3}  or  ℍ 4 \mathbb{H}^{4} . The reflection hyperplanes (i.e. fixed points of conjugates of generators of  W S ′′ W_{S^{\prime\prime}} ) cut  X X  into a simplicial complex, which is isomorphic to the Coxeter complex  𝖢 S ′′ \mathsf{C}_{S^{\prime\prime}} . Let  K S ′′ K_{S^{\prime\prime}}  be the fundamental domain of the action  W S ′′ ↷ 𝖢 S ′′ W_{S^{\prime\prime}}\curvearrowright\mathsf{C}_{S^{\prime\prime}} , which is a simplex with an inherit metric from  X X . Now we identify  K S ′ K_{S^{\prime}}  with  K S ′′ K_{S^{\prime\prime}}  with the vertex of type  s ^ \hat{s}  in  K S ′ K_{S^{\prime}}  with  s ∈ S ′ s\in S^{\prime}  is identified with the vertex of type  f ^ ​ ( s ) \hat{f}(s)  in  K S ′′ K_{S^{\prime\prime}} . This gives a metric on  K S ′ K_{S^{\prime}} , hence induces a piecewise Euclidean or hyperbolic metric in  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}} . 
 
 
 
 Conjecture A.3 . 
 
 Let  A S A_{S}  be an Artin group and suppose  S S  contains a subset  S ′ S^{\prime}  such that  A S ′ A_{S^{\prime}}  is almost spherical. Then  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  with the metric in Definition  A.2  is CAT ( 0 ) (0)  when  S ′′ S^{\prime\prime}  in Definition  A.2  is Euclidean, and is CAT ( − 1 ) (-1)  when  S ′′ S^{\prime\prime}  in Definition  A.2  is either of type  [ 3 , 5 , 3 ] [3,5,3]  or  [ 5 , 3 , 3 , 3 ] [5,3,3,3] . Hence  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  is contractible. 
 
 
 
 To prove Conjecture  A.3 , it suffices to show  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  is simply-connected, which follows from Lemma  3.4 , and the link of each vertex is CAT ( 1 ) (1) . The link condition is rather challenging to verify, especially when  dim ( Δ S , S ′ ) ≥ 2 \dim(\Delta_{S,S^{\prime}})\geq 2 . We refer to  [ EM04 ,  EM02 ,  McC09 ]  for some related discussion. 
 
 
 Due to the difficulty of verifying the link condition in the CAT ( 0 ) (0)  setting, we propose an alternative metric on  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  for most types of almost spherical sets  S ′ S^{\prime} , which is not exactly CAT ( 0 ) (0) , but has strong flavor of non-positive curvature. 
 
 
 Definition A.4   (Proposed metric II) . 
 
 Let  S , S ′ , Δ S , S ′ , K S ′ S,S^{\prime},\Delta_{S,S^{\prime}},K_{S^{\prime}}  be as in Definition  A.2 . We assume the type of  S ′ S^{\prime}  does not belong to  { F ~ 4 , E ~ 6 , E ~ 7 , E ~ 8 , [ 3 , 5 , 3 ] , [ 5 , 3 , 3 , 3 ] } \{\widetilde{F}_{4},\widetilde{E}_{6},\widetilde{E}_{7},\widetilde{E}_{8},[3,5,3],[5,3,3,3]\} .
By Lemma  A.1  (2), there is an Artin group  A S ′′ A_{S^{\prime\prime}}  dominated by  A S ′ A_{S^{\prime}}  under a bijection  f : S ′ → S ′′ f:S^{\prime}\to S^{\prime\prime}  such that the type of  A S ′′ A_{S^{\prime\prime}}  belongs to  { G ~ 2 , A ~ n , B ~ n , C ~ n , D ~ n } \{\widetilde{G}_{2},\widetilde{A}_{n},\widetilde{B}_{n},\widetilde{C}_{n},\widetilde{D}_{n}\} . Let  K S ′′ K_{S^{\prime\prime}}  be as in Definition  A.2 . By the same procedure as in Definition  A.2 , it suffices to metrize  K S ′′ K_{S^{\prime\prime}} . 
 
 
 If  S ′′ S^{\prime\prime}  is of type  G ~ 2 \widetilde{G}_{2} , then we metrize in the same we as before. If  S ′′ S^{\prime\prime}  is of type  B ~ n , C ~ n , D ~ n \widetilde{B}_{n},\widetilde{C}_{n},\widetilde{D}_{n} , then  W S ′′ W_{S^{\prime\prime}}  acts properly and cocompactly on  𝔼 n \mathbb{E}^{n}  as before, and this action is isometric with respect to the  ℓ ∞ \ell^{\infty} -metric. We equipped  K S ′′ K_{S^{\prime\prime}}  with the induced  ℓ ∞ \ell^{\infty} -metric from  𝔼 n \mathbb{E}^{n} . If  S ′′ S^{\prime\prime}  is of type  A ~ n \widetilde{A}_{n} , then the Coxeter complex  𝖢 S ′′ \mathsf{C}_{S^{\prime\prime}}  can be identified with the subset  H = { x ∈ 𝔼 n + 1 ∣ x 1 + x 2 + ⋯ + x n + 1 = 0 } H=\{x\in\mathbb{E}^{n+1}\mid x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{n+1}=0\}  cut out by the hyperplanes  x i − x j ∈ ℤ x_{i}-x_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}  for  1 ≤ i ≠ j ≤ n + 1 1\leq i\neq j\leq n+1 , with a fundamental domain  K S ′′ K_{S^{\prime\prime}}  be the set  { x 1 ≤ x 2 ≤ ⋯ ≤ x n ≤ x 1 + 1 } \{x_{1}\leq x_{2}\leq\cdots\leq x_{n}\leq x_{1}+1\}  in  H H . We equip  𝔼 n + 1 \mathbb{E}^{n+1}  with  ℓ ∞ \ell^{\infty} -metric, and metrize  K S ′′ K_{S^{\prime\prime}}  with the induced metric. 
 
 
 
 The idea of domination allows us to “pretend”  S ′ S^{\prime}  is of Euclidean type, and metrize with  ℓ ∞ \ell^{\infty} -metric, even if it is actually of hyperbolic type. However, as  [ 3 , 5 , 3 ] [3,5,3]  and  [ 5 , 3 , 3 , 3 ] [5,3,3,3]  do not dominate any Euclidean type, we need new candidates of metric in these two cases. In the special case of  S = S ′ ∈ { A ~ n , C ~ n } S=S^{\prime}\in\{\widetilde{A}_{n},\widetilde{C}_{n}\} , the metric in Definition  A.4  was defined in  [ Hae21 ] . 
 
 
 Here is a more concrete description of the  ℓ ∞ \ell^{\infty} -metric on  K S ′′ K_{S^{\prime\prime}}  when  S ′′ S^{\prime\prime}  is of type  { B ~ n , C ~ n , D ~ n } \{\widetilde{B}_{n},\widetilde{C}_{n},\widetilde{D}_{n}\} . In the case of type  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n} , the Coxeter complex  𝖢 S ′′ \mathsf{C}_{S^{\prime\prime}}  is isomorphic to  𝔼 n \mathbb{E}^{n}  cut out by the hyperplanes  x i ± x j ∈ ℤ x_{i}\pm x_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}  and  x i ∈ 1 2 ​ ℤ x_{i}\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z} . Then the fundamental domain  K S ′′ K_{S^{\prime\prime}}  can be chosen to be the subset of  𝔼 n \mathbb{E}^{n}  defined by  0 ≤ x 1 ≤ x 2 ≤ ⋯ ≤ x n ≤ 1 2 0\leq x_{1}\leq x_{2}\leq\cdots\leq x_{n}\leq\frac{1}{2} , equipped with the  ℓ ∞ \ell^{\infty}  metric. If  S ′′ S^{\prime\prime}  is of type  B ~ n \widetilde{B}_{n} , then the subdivision of the associated Coxeter complex  𝖢 S ′′ \mathsf{C}_{S^{\prime\prime}}  as in Definition  3.30  is isomorphic to the Coxeter complex of type  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n} , thus a type  B ~ n \widetilde{B}_{n}  fundamental domain is the union of two copies of type  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n}  fundamental domain along an appropriate codimension 1 face. If  S ′′ S^{\prime\prime}  is of type  D ~ n \widetilde{D}_{n} , then subdivision of the associated Coxeter complex  𝖢 S ′′ \mathsf{C}_{S^{\prime\prime}}  as in Definition  3.33  is isomorphic to the Coxeter complex of type  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n} . Hence a  D ~ n \widetilde{D}_{n} -fundamental domain is the union of four copies of  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n}  fundamental domain. 
 
 
 Recall that a  geodesic bicombing  in a metric space  X X , is the assignment of a geodesic segment from  x x  to  y y , for each order pair of points  ( x , y ) (x,y)  in  X X . We do not require the geodesic segment from  x x  to  y y  is the same as the geodesic segment from  y y  to  x x . Note that in Definition  A.4 , each top-dimensional simplex has geodesic bicombing coming from the linear structure on each simplex. The hope is that these geodesic bicombing fit together to form a geodesic bicombing on the whole space such that the bicombing varies continuously with respect to their endpoints, hence one can show  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  is contractible by using the geodesic contraction. 
 
 
 Conjecture A.5 . 
 
 Let  A S A_{S}  be an Artin group and suppose  S S  contains a subset  S ′ S^{\prime}  which is almost spherical, but its type is not contained in  { F ~ 4 , E ~ 6 , E ~ 7 , E ~ 8 , [ 3 , 5 , 3 ] , [ 5 , 3 , 3 , 3 ] } \{\widetilde{F}_{4},\widetilde{E}_{6},\widetilde{E}_{7},\widetilde{E}_{8},[3,5,3],[5,3,3,3]\} . Then  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  equipped with the metric in Definition  A.4  is a metric space with convex geodesic bicombing in the sense of  [ DL15 ] , hence contractible. 
 
 
 
 We conjecture that similar to the situation of proving Conjecture  A.3 , one is reduced to check an appropriate form of link condition. Again checking this link condition will be the most challenging part. Though we believe the link condition to check in the context of Conjecture  A.5  will be much simpler than Conjecture  A.3  - when  S ′′ S^{\prime\prime}  is not of type  G ~ 2 \widetilde{G}_{2} , then link condition should only involve cycles in the 1-skeleton of the link up to length  6 6 . Combined with some existing link condition in  [ Hae22 ] , we know the following. 
 
 
 Lemma A.6 . 
 
 Suppose  Λ \Lambda  is a connected Dynkin diagram, and  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is an admissible subgraph of  Λ \Lambda  in the sense of Definition  3.13 . Suppose  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is almost spherical with vertex set  S ′ S^{\prime}  and  Λ \Lambda  has vertex set  S S . Suppose the type of  S ′ S^{\prime}  is not one of  { G ~ 2 , F ~ 4 , E ~ 6 , E ~ 7 , E ~ 8 , [ 3 , 5 , 3 ] , [ 5 , 3 , 3 , 3 ] } \{\widetilde{G}_{2},\widetilde{F}_{4},\widetilde{E}_{6},\widetilde{E}_{7},\widetilde{E}_{8},[3,5,3],[5,3,3,3]\} . Then there is a criterion only involving cycles in the 1-skeleton up to length 6 in the link of each vertex of  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  such that as long as such link criterion is satisfied, then  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  is contractible. More precisely, 
 
 1.  
 
 if  S ′ S^{\prime}  dominates type  A ~ n \widetilde{A}_{n}  (in which case the Dynkin diagram of  S ′ S^{\prime}  must be a cycle, hence elements in  S ′ S^{\prime}  has a natural cyclic order), and then we need to check  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}} , viewed as a simplicial complex of type  S ′ S^{\prime} , satisfies the assumptions of Theorem  3.28 ; 
 
 2.  
 
 if  S ′ S^{\prime}  dominates type  C ~ n \widetilde{C}_{n}  (in which case the Dynkin diagram of  S ′ S^{\prime}  is a line, hence  S ′ S^{\prime}  has two linear orders), then we need to check if  S ′ S^{\prime}  is equipped with one of these linear orders, then  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  satisfies the assumptions of Theorem  3.27 ; 
 
 3.  
 
 if  S ′ S^{\prime}  dominates type  B ~ n \widetilde{B}_{n}  (in which case the Dynkin diagram of  S ′ S^{\prime}  is isomorphic to Figure  5  left), then we need to check assumptions of Proposition  3.32  are satisfied; 
 
 4.  
 
 if  S ′ S^{\prime}  dominates type  D ~ n \widetilde{D}_{n}  (in which case the Dynkin diagram of  S ′ S^{\prime}  is isomorphic to Figure  5  right), then we need to check assumptions of Proposition  3.34  are satisfied. 
 
 
 
 
 
 In the case when  S ′ S^{\prime}  is of type  G ~ 2 \widetilde{G}_{2} , there is an obvious link condition to check coming from the CAT ( 0 ) (0)  link condition. 
 
 
 Note that Lemma  A.6  requires  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  to be an admissible subgraph of  Λ \Lambda . If  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is not an admissible subgraph of  Λ \Lambda , then the link criterion in Lemma  A.6  does not apply directly. For example, in Case 2 of Lemma  A.6 , if  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is not an admissible, then  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  with the relation induced from the linear order from  S ′ S^{\prime}  is not a poset. So Theorem  3.27  does not apply directly. Similar problems happen with the other cases. This leads to us to formulate the following somewhat less precise conjecture. 
 
 
 Conjecture A.7 . 
 
 Under the same assumption of Lemma  A.6 , even if  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is not admissible in  Λ \Lambda , there still exists a criterion only involving cycles in the 1-skeleton up to length 6 in the link of each vertex of  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  such that as long as such link criterion is satisfied, then  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  is contractible. 
 
 
 
 For the remaining types of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime} , we ask the following. 
 
 
 Question A.8 . 
 
 Suppose  Λ \Lambda  is a connected Dynkin diagram, and  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is an induced subgraph of  Λ \Lambda  in the sense of Definition  3.13 . Suppose the type of  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  is one of  { F ~ 4 , E ~ 6 , E ~ 7 , E ~ 8 \{\widetilde{F}_{4},\widetilde{E}_{6},\widetilde{E}_{7},\widetilde{E}_{8} ,  [ 3 , 5 , 3 ] , [ 5 , 3 , 3 , 3 ] } [3,5,3],[5,3,3,3]\} . Is it true that there is a criterion only involving cycles in the 1-skeleton up to a certain length in the link of each vertex of  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  such that as long as such link criterion is satisfied, then  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  is contractible? 
 
 
 
 
 Appendix B  Conjectures on cycles in relative Artin complexes 

 
 We list two conjectures which are necessary for the strategy in  [ Hua23 ]  to work. We refer to Section  3.2  and Section  3.3  for related terminologies. 
 
 
 Conjecture B.1 . 
 
 Suppose  A S A_{S}  is an irreducible spherical Artin group with Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda . Let  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  be a linear subdiagram of  Λ \Lambda  with consecutive vertices  { s i } i = 1 n \{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}  such that the edge between  s n − 1 s_{n-1}  and  s n s_{n}  has label  ≥ 4 \geq 4 . Then the vertex set of  Δ Λ , Λ ′ \Delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}  equipped with the relation induced from  s 1 < s 2 < ⋯ < s n s_{1}<s_{2}<\cdots<s_{n}  (as in Definition  3.12 ) is a bowtie free and upward flag poset. 
 
 
 
 The bowtie free part is already known, and is a consequence of  [ Hua23 , Proposition 2.8] . For the upward flag part, the case when  A S = A S ′ A_{S}=A_{S^{\prime}}  is type  B n B_{n}  follows from  [ Hae21 ] ; the case that  A S A_{S}  is of type  F 4 F_{4}  and  A S ′ A_{S^{\prime}}  is of type  B 3 B_{3}  follows from Proposition  4.2 ; the case  A S A_{S}  being type  H 3 H_{3}  follows from Theorem  7.1 . The remaining case of the conjecture is the upward flag part of  A S A_{S}  being type  H 4 H_{4} . 
 
 
 Conjecture B.2 . 
 
 Suppose  A S A_{S}  is an irreducible spherical Artin group. Let  S ′ ⊂ S S^{\prime}\subset S  such that  A S ′ A_{S^{\prime}}  has Dynkin diagram isomorphic to the type  D n D_{n}  Dynkin diagram for  n ≥ 3 n\geq 3  (the isomorphism does not need to preserve edge labels). Let  { b i } i = 1 n + 1 \{b_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n+1}  be vertices in  S ′ S^{\prime}  as in Figure  5  left. Then the  ( b 1 , b 2 ) (b_{1},b_{2}) -subdivision of  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  (in the sense of Definition  3.30 ) is a bowtie free and downward flag poset. 
 
 
 
 Note that we specifically allow the case when the Dynkind diagram  A S ′ A_{S^{\prime}}  is isomorphic to the type  D 3 D_{3}  diagram (though edge labels might not be preserved). While  D 3 D_{3}  diagram is the same as  A 3 A_{3}  diagram, but we are considering a subdivision of  Δ S , S ′ \Delta_{S,S^{\prime}}  by viewing the Dynkin diagram as  D 3 D_{3}  rather than  A 3 A_{3} , as explained in Definition  3.30 . 
 
 
 Again the bowtie free part follows from  [ Hua23 , Proposition 2.8] . The downward flag part is known when  n = 3 n=3 , and  A S A_{S}  is of type  A n , B n , H 3 , F 4 A_{n},B_{n},H_{3},F_{4}  - this is a combination of Lemma  11.2 , Theorem  5.6 , Theorem  7.1  and Proposition  4.2 . By Theorem  3.16 , the downward flag part is also known when  A S A_{S}  is of type  D n D_{n} , and  S ′ = { b 1 , b 2 , b 3 } S^{\prime}=\{b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}\}  in Figure  5 . The downward flag part is true when  A S = A S ′ A_{S}=A_{S^{\prime}}  is of type  D 4 D_{4} , see Theorem  10.1 . The remaining cases are open. 
 
 
 Conjecture  B.2  generalizes an earlier conjecture of Haettel, in the special case of  A S = A S ′ A_{S}=A_{S^{\prime}}  are both of type  D n D_{n} . 
 
 
 Our plan is to first establish Conjecture  B.1  and Conjecture  B.2 . Then use a suitable propagation argument to establish the following. 
 
 
 Conjecture B.3 . 
 
 Conjecture  B.1  and Conjecture  B.2  are still true for any  A S A_{S}  with connected Dynkin diagram  Λ \Lambda , and  A S ′ A_{S^{\prime}}  corresponds to an admissible full subgraph of  Λ \Lambda  satisfying the requirements in Conjecture  B.1  and Conjecture  B.2 . 
 
 
 
 Then one can use Conjecture  B.3  together with Lemma  A.6  to establish Conjecture  A.5  partially. The reason for the word “partially” is that Conjecture  B.3 , Theorem  3.28 , Theorem  3.27 , Proposition  3.32  and Proposition  3.34  require the subgraph  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  inside  Λ \Lambda  to be admissible, this will ensure the relation on the vertices of the associated complex to be a poset. Actually, the poset part of Conjecture  B.1  and Conjecture  B.2  will fail if the subdiagram  Λ ′ \Lambda^{\prime}  of  Λ \Lambda  corresponding to  A S ′ A_{S^{\prime}}  is not admissible. In this case, we need to find suitable replacements in order to prove Conjecture  A.5  fully.

      methods listed below:

      may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
      should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
      all.
